# Chapter 5 Neural Network Approaches for Transportation

N. R. GAYATHIRI^([³](#fn3))

## Abstract

In the current generation, neural networks are used in various sectors like education, finance, speech recognition, face detection, aerospace, automotive, transport, stock market prediction, transportation, etc. Transportation system plays a vital role in economic development. Sustainable and acceptable artificial neural networks (ANNs) techniques are used to make the transportation system intelligent. We use neural network approaches for various transportation issues like traffic forecasting, route planning, vehicle number plate detection, parking space identification, vehicle density, travel time, autonomous vehicle, vehicle location, vehicle speed, driver behavior, traffic management, etc. The neural networks algorithm helps to make the decision like human beings. So, this chapter presents intelligence in the transportation system through the efficient implementation of neural networks. Neural Network simplified design configuration is the added advantage for making the intelligent transportation system.

## 5.1 Introduction

The neural network is a computer model with interrelated nodes that have characteristics like the human neurons to process the information. Neural networks can pick out the hidden styles and correlations in raw facts, group, and classify data, and as time go through, it learns and improves with the assistance of algorithms \[[8](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_8)\].

Basic functions of neural networks:

1.  **Classification:** Arrange patterns or datasets into predetermined classes.
2.  **Prediction:** Generate the anticipated output from a given input.
3.  **Clustering:** Identify a unique characteristic of the data and categorize them without any prior knowledge of the data.
4.  **Associating:** Neural networks are trained to remember patterns and models. When we show an unknown version of a model, the network associates it with the most comparable version of its memory and returns to the last \[[14](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_14)\].

## 5.2 How Do Neural Networks Operate?

Neural networks are made up of a large number of artificial neurons, which are called nodes that are organized in a sequence of layers. The input layer is the first layer that accepts inputs in multiple different formats supplied by the programmer. There is one more layer called the hidden layer present between the input and output layer. The hidden layer is used to perform all computations in order to find hidden features and patterns. Using the hidden layer, the input passes through a series of transformations which ultimately leads to an output which is displayed using the output layer.

Input data from the input layer moves into hidden layers for processing. In the processing stage, every input data is assigned with appropriate weights. Each input data is multiplied by its weights. Then, summing of input-weight takes place. Bias is the additional parameter added along with a weighted sum of input to adjust the output. Bias added weighted sum of input is passed to the activation function. The activation function decides the output that the node is going to generate based on the input data. Finally, the output is retrieved from the output layer. If the expected output is not generated, back propagation has to be done.

Neural networks are an example of a supervised learning algorithm and aim to approximate the function represented by the data. This is accomplished by calculating the error between projected and expected outputs during the training phase and minimizing it ([Figure 5.1](#ch5.xhtml#fig5_1)).

![Figure 5.1 Workflow of neural networks.](images/fig5_1_B.jpg)

[Figure 5.1 Workflow of neural networks.](#ch5.xhtml#R_fig5_1)

## 5.3 Working Process of Single Node

Here the product inputs (X[1], X[2], X[3], …, X[n]) in the input layer and weights (W[1], W[2], W[3], …, W[n]) in the hidden layer are summed with bias (b) and then acted on by an activation function f(y). In a neural network, the activation function is the most important aspect in determining whether or not a neuron in the network will be activated and transported to the next layer. This essentially implies that it will determine whether or not the input to the neural network is significant to the prediction process. The activation functions are also known as threshold or transformation. Due to their differentiable nature, it aids in the normalization of the output between 0 and 1 or –1 to 1 in the back propagation process ([Figure 5.2](#ch5.xhtml#fig5_2)).

![Figure 5.2 Workflow of single node in neural networks.](images/fig5_2_B.jpg)

[Figure 5.2 Workflow of single node in neural networks.](#ch5.xhtml#R_fig5_2)

The different types of activation functions:

*   Binary step;
*   Leaky ReLU;
*   Softsign;
*   SoftMax;
*   Swish;
*   ReLU;
*   Parameterized ReLU;
*   Tanh;
*   Exponential linear unit; • Sigmoid;
*   Linear.

## 5.4 Optimization Algorithms

The approach used to carry out the learning process is known as the training (or learning) strategy. In order to produce the best possible outcomes, the neural network is trained using the training technique. The manner in which the parameters of the neural network are adjusted determines the type of training. Neural network can learn from its past experience. Learning process is carried out by the Optimization algorithm. Optimization algorithms are of different types based on memory, processing speed and other characteristics:

*   Gradient descent;
*   Adam;
*   SGD with momentum;
*   Adaptive gradient (AdaGrad);
*   Newton method;
*   AdaDelta;
*   Quasi-Newton method;
*   Conjugate gradient;
*   Levenberg-Marquardt algorithm;
*   Nesterov accelerated gradient (NAG);
*   Stochastic gradient descent (SGD);
*   Mini batch stochastic gradient descent (MB-SGD).

## 5.5 Ntelligent Transport System Applications

Various types of applications of neural networks in the Intelligent Transportation System are presented in the chapter \[[2](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_2)\]:

*   Traffic light recognition;
*   Traffic sign recognition;
*   Traffic forecasting and control;
*   Evaluation of traffic parameters;
*   Traffic signal timing detection;
*   Vehicle classification;
*   Driver behavior recognition;
*   Road damage detection;
*   Lane/turn recognition;
*   Prediction/understanding traffic scene;
*   Vehicle attribute detection;
*   Maintenance of transport infrastructure;
*   Autonomous vehicles;
*   Vehicle parking;
*   Predict bus arrival time;
*   Travel time prediction;
*   Detecting vehicle location and estimation of the vehicle speed on the road;
*   License plate detection;
*   Pedestrian detection.

## 5.6 Classification Of Neural Networks

Based on the mathematical model, principles, and parameters, neural networks are classified. Each neural network has different learning processes and also has its own advantages and disadvantages. The most common types of neural networks in use today and for what they are used are discussed.

### 5.6.1 Feed-Forward Neural Network

In this type of neural network, data travels from the input layer to the output layer through hidden layers (if any) only in the forward direction. The data passes only in the forward and never backward direction, so cycles are not formed in the network. Feed-forward neural network does not remember the past as there is no input memory. So, it does not predict the future. It remembers only the training.

#### 5.6.1.1 Applications: Recognition of Face and Computer Vision ([Figures 5.3](#ch5.xhtml#fig5_3) and [5.4](#ch5.xhtml#fig5_4))

![Figure 5.3 Workflow of single node in neural networks.](images/fig5_3_B.jpg)

[Figure 5.3 Workflow of single node in neural networks.](#ch5.xhtml#R_fig5_3)

![Figure 5.4 Feed-forward neural network (hidden layer).](images/fig5_4_B.jpg)

[Figure 5.4 Feed-forward neural network (hidden layer).](#ch5.xhtml#R_fig5_4)

#### 5.6.1.2 Single Layer Perceptron

If the hidden layer does not exist in the feed forward neural network, then it is called single layer perceptron. Input data is assigned with random weights because there is no past knowledge. The product of input-weight is summed up. Threshold value is fixed for each perceptron. The perceptron is activated only if the summation value of input weight is above the threshold value. If the received output is not the same as the predicted output, then weights assigned to the input data are changed to get the desired output ([Figure 5.5](#ch5.xhtml#fig5_5)).

![Figure 5.5 Single layer perceptron.](images/fig5_5_B.jpg)

[Figure 5.5 Single layer perceptron.](#ch5.xhtml#R_fig5_5)

#### 5.6.1.3 Multilayer Perceptron

If an arbitrary number of hidden layers exist in the Feed Forward Neural Network, then it is called a Multi-Layer Perceptron. Input data from the input layer passes through many hidden layers to process the input data. From the hidden layer processed data goes to the output layer to display the output. In this process, hidden layers are the computational engine. Multilayer perceptron is trained to relate the input-output correlation. To get the predicted output parameters, bias, and weights are adjusted. Adjustment is done by back propagation method ([Figure 5.6](#ch5.xhtml#fig5_6)).

![Figure 5.6 Multi-layer perceptron.](images/fig5_6_B.jpg)

[Figure 5.6 Multi-layer perceptron.](#ch5.xhtml#R_fig5_6)

### 5.6.2 Convolutional Neural Network (Cnn)

Another type of neural network is convolutional neural network (CNN) which is formed by different layers. Each layer is explained below ([Figure 5.7](#ch5.xhtml#fig5_7)):

![Figure 5.7 Convolutional neural network.](images/fig5_7_B.jpg)

[Figure 5.7 Convolutional neural network.](#ch5.xhtml#R_fig5_7)

1.  **Input Layer:** It is to feed the input (image) data into the model for processing.
2.  **Convolutional Layer:** This mines the different features from the image data.
3.  **Pooling Layer:** It is used to decrease the computational factors and it is present between the successive convolution layers.
4.  **ReLU Layer:** It is present for removing negative pixels by zero (non-linearity).
5.  **Fully Connected Layer:** It is used to connect the neurons present in one layer to the next layer by a multi-layer perceptron. Every neuron learns from the past experience and that learning helps to predict the output.

The foremost application area of CNN is image recognition and image classification. If the desired output is not achieved, then do back propagation by adjusting the weight and bias to get the predicted output. Less preprocessing is the main advantage of convolution neural networks. Less preprocessing is achieved by kernels (set of filters).

### 5.6.3 Recurrent Neural Network (Rnn)

Each node present in the recurrent neural network (RNN) acts as a memory cell, to store the output of the previous layer. Now, the input for the next layer will be the previous layer output. RNN uses the above method to predict the outcome of a layer. This type of neural network uses front propagation first and stores all processed information for future use. If the predicted output is not the same as the received output, then we want to make a neural network to self-learn to correct the mistake by back propagation method. The main application of RNNs is text to speech conversion ([Figure 5.8](#ch5.xhtml#fig5_8)).

![Figure 5.8 Recurrent neural network (RNN).](images/fig5_8_B.jpg)

[Figure 5.8 Recurrent neural network (RNN).](#ch5.xhtml#R_fig5_8)

### 5.6.4 Graph Neural Network (Gnn)

Graph neural networks (GNNs) resemble the graph data structure. If two nodes are interrelated, then those two nodes should maintain its neighbor nodes information (embeddings). This is achieved by GNNs by connecting the nodes through edges. This neighborhood information is used to solve many problems like node and edge prediction. The GNN is widely used in our day-to-day applications. Its importance is remarkable one. Social networks, Google maps, Chemical compound linking, recommender systems, and route map in transportation are quite a few applications where GNNs are used.

### 5.6.5 Working of Graph Neural Networks (GNNs)

The aim of GNN is that each node should learn about the other nodes in the network. In the above figure, Node A and Node C are the neighbor nodes for Node B. Node B collects Node A and Node C information. After that, Node B adds its own information and passes whole embeddings (A, B, C) to the main Node A. The above process continues until all the nodes in the network know about other nodes. The same process is repeated for Node C and Node D. Now main node A contains whole graph embeddings ([Figure 5.9](#ch5.xhtml#fig5_9)).

![Figure 5.9 Graph neural network (GNN).](images/fig5_9_B.jpg)

[Figure 5.9 Graph neural network (GNN).](#ch5.xhtml#R_fig5_9)

## 5.7 Convolutional Neural Network (Cnn) in Transportation

### 5.7.1 Traffic Characteristics Prediction

Traffic is the major problem in transportation. Using the intelligence of transportation, traffic can be reduced by predicting the traffic characteristics. This prediction will help the drivers to choose routes more efficiently. Vehicle speed, traffic density, flow, and conditions are the main traffic characteristics \[[1](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_1)\].

The major problem in the traffic prediction is irregular traffic density. We cannot predict the time accurately for heavy traffic. It varies from time to time. But irregular traffic can be predicted with high accuracy using deep learning methods. Instead of using parametric and statistical methods, recently non-parametric methods give higher prediction for traffic flow.

### 5.7.2 Traffic Incidents Inference

Accidents are quite common in some locations due to heavy traffic. Heavy traffic results in traffic incident risk. We want to predict the traffic incident risks based on the traffic parameters in the location. Driver Speed, Weather, Vehicle Flow, Human Mobility, Geographical position of the location, Traffic flow are some of the parameters considered for traffic incidents risk. But the above parameters will differ from place to place and time to time for the same location. All the model steps have to be stored in the data pool to predict incident severity in the future.

### 5.7.3 Vehicle Identification

Automatic vehicle identification plays a vital role in toll gate and vehicle parking. Vehicles are identified with the number plates which are unique for each vehicle.

Number plate recognition becomes difficult due to the parameters like Low quality camera, less lightening and blurriness due to vehicle movement and number plates are hidden by other vehicles. To overcome the above drawback, a deep learning visual model is used. Reading Vehicle number plates are automatically recognized by the combination of support vector machine (SVM) and CNN. In case of, similar number plate Siamese neural network is used \[[1](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_1)\]. CNNs play a vital role in recognizing license plate, vehicle color, and vehicle type classification.

### 5.7.4 Traffic Signal Timing

Automatic traffic signal change is very important to reduce traffic and accidents. Based on the time, traffic signal lights are changed. In order to reduce the risk, traffic signal lights have to be changed based on the traffic density. This can be done by deep CNNs. In the first stage CNN extracts the raw live data like vehicle current position, speed of the vehicles and other objects mobility. In the second stage, raw data is given to the deep reinforcement learning (RL) network. This reinforcement network is responsible for predicting the automatic change of traffic signal lights (red, green, yellow), waiting time and safe left turn. In this way traffic signal light is changed according to the density of the vehicle not based on the time \[[1](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_1)\].

### 5.7.5 Ride Sharing and Public Transportation

Frequent Public transportation has to be provided to the places where passenger demand is more. Similarly, when there is no passenger demand, the frequency of public transportation has to be reduced. Vehicle routing has to take place as per the passenger flow. CNN is used to predict the travel mode, passenger flow, passenger demand prediction \[[1](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_1)\].

### 5.7.6 Visual Recognition Tasks

Autonomous vehicles (AVs) are the booming technology in transportation. Driverless vehicles depend mainly on visual recognition. Camera image-based systems along with the sensors is used for object detection like obstacle detection \[[1](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_1)\].

### 5.7.7 Rate the Road Lane-Based on Incoming Traffic

CNN is used to rate the road lanes based on the incoming traffic. The traffic signal lights are changed based on the road lane classification rate (heavy, moderate, less traffic), which is more useful for traffic management. The road lane videos which is taken from 360° camera is given as input in the form of video frames to the CNN. A large amount of video frames is captured by traffic cameras and pass the video frames as training data to the neural network for better prediction. The output of CNN is analyzed with the traffic signals and the road lanes with high traffic rate value is allotted with more traffic signal light time \[[12](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_12)\].

### 5.7.8 License Plate Recognition

Identification of License plate is difficult task due to various shape, font, color, and light effects. The alphabets and numbers in the license plate is segmented using a clustering algorithm. Then, CNN is used to find the alphabets and numbers in the license plate \[[11](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_11)\].

## 5.8 Other Applications of Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNS)

Obstacle detection, vehicle number plate identification, vehicle color and model detection, traffic sign recognition and auto changing, traffic light recognition, free lane detection, labeling the scene around place, traffic scene segmentation, road crack detection are some applications where CNNs is used for intelligence transportation.

## 5.9 Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNS) in Selfdriving Cars

Decision process is autonomous in self-driving vehicles. The concept behind the automatic decision-making process is deep learning algorithms. These algorithms process the data, which is collected from different sensors like RADAR, GPS, LiDAR. Among the deep learning algorithms, CNNs are the most important algorithm for image recognition and classification. Self-driving car uses CNNs for recognition and classification of the various parts of the road and also for taking the correct decision \[[3](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_3)\]. The main components of driverless cars are explained in subsections.

### 5.9.1 erception

To drive the car safely, the driver has to recognize and classify the things around the car environment. Recognition and classification of things takes place through the driver’s eyes. In the driverless car, cameras, and sensors are used to recognize and classify the objects like pedestrians, parking palace, traffic signal lights, road signs, road turns and parallel vehicles. In order to avoid accidents, the exact distance between driverless cars and the surrounding objects are to be calculated automatically. Based on the distance calculation, the car has to slow down automatically.

For the self-driving cars, sensors like LiDAR, Camera, and RADAR act as human eyes for good perception. [Figure 5.10](#ch5.xhtml#fig5_10) shows the AI powered autonomous car perception-planning-action to take the driving decision.

![Figure 5.10 AI powered autonomous car perception-planning-action.](images/fig5_10_B.jpg)

[Figure 5.10 AI powered autonomous car perception-planning-action.](#ch5.xhtml#R_fig5_10)

### 5.9.2 Camera

The high-quality camera acts as the human eye for driver less cars. Camera is used for segmentation, classification, and localization. High resolution Camera captures the surrounding environment images of the car more accurately \[[5](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_5)\].

### 5.9.3 Lidar

The abbreviation for LiDAR is light detection and ranging. From the Lidar Sensor, laser light is fired. The distance between driverless cars and other objects is measured by how far the laser beam is reflected by the surrounding objects. Camera captures the surrounding images. Combination of camera and LiDAR sensor gives the detailed information of surrounding objects around the car. This detailed information is given as input to the deep neural networks for taking the safest decision. This is very useful in multi-exit driving conditions.

### 5.9.4 Radars

In the LiDAR sensor, the distance between driverless cars and other objects is measured by laser beam. But Radio detection and ranging (RADAR) uses radio signals to calculate the distance between driverless cars and other objects. Even high-quality cameras cannot capture some objects, but uncaptured objects are detected by radio signals \[[5](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_5)\].

### 5.9.5 Localization

Location and inclination of the car changes as the vehicle moves. The Localization algorithm uses consecutive frames of the video for calculating the Location and inclination of the car called visual odometry (VO). Simultaneous localization and mapping (SLAM) algorithm are used to find the location and inclination of close vehicle by referring to the previous frame.

### 5.9.6 Prediction

The high-quality camera in the self-driving car gives the 360-view image. All the camera images, Data from the LiDAR and RADAR sensors are given as input data to the deep neural network and then it processes the input data for decision making. Processed Data in different formats are used for object detection, classification, localization, and prediction \[[5](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_5)\].

Due to the more hidden layers in deep learning networks, data from different sources are processed accurately for making the correct decisions. Deep layers help the car to take the accurate inference for changing the route with actions like speed slowly, halt, brake, and taking the different route. Deep learning is used for computer vision and perception \[[5](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_5)\].

### 5.9.7 Decision-Making

Driverless cars should take the decision as the human being even in unexpected situations. Driverless cars should take the accurate decision like slowing down the car, changing the route and turning the car, when the person is crossing the road all of a sudden. Sensors are used to collect the data from the different sources to make the correct decision. Position of the car and car movements are decided by deep learning algorithm \[[5](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_5)\].

Deep RL is used for the decision-making process. The Markov decision process is used for future decisions and route planning. Networks learn from the past information to predict the future behavior. Route planning, Behavior Arbitration, Motion Planning and Vehicle Control are the different components used in self-driving cars for taking decisions \[[5](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_5)\].

### 5.9.8 Route Planning

The destination is fed into the car by the passenger. The driverless car should take the best route with less traffic, good road conditions, and less time. If the obstacle is present, the car has to take the decision by stopping the car or changing the car inclination.

### 5.9.9 Behavior Arbitration

Markov decision process decides the route planning. But the route plan has to be changed if there is uncertainty like sudden vehicle cross or natural calamities \[[5](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_5)\].

### 5.9.10 Motion Planning

Final destination is reached by the passenger through the Motion planning activities like route changing and speed change. Motion planning is dynamically changed based on the current surroundings of the car \[[5](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_5)\]. Self-driving car architecture is given in [Figure 5.11](#ch5.xhtml#fig5_11).

![Figure 5.11 Self-driving car architecture.](images/fig5_11_B.jpg)

[Figure 5.11 Self-driving car architecture.](#ch5.xhtml#R_fig5_11)

### 5.9.11 Vehicle Control

The passenger has to reach the final destination with utmost safety. When the car speed is increased or decreased suddenly, the vehicle speed should be in control ensuring the surrounding safety.

CNN extracts the hidden features/pattern from the data collected from the high-quality camera and sensors. The neural network goes deeper by adding more hidden layers to extract the complex features like the shape of the tire, and surrounding objects. Because of the above reason, CNN acts as the main algorithm \[[4](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_4)\]. Nvidia, Tesla, Google Waymo are the big giant companies involved in making self-driving cars using CNNs.

## 5.10 Graph Neural Network (GNN) for Traffic Forecasting

Graph attention and graph convolutional networks are the important types of GNNs used for traffic forecasting problems. Speed, traffic flow, demand, and passenger flow forecasting are the major traffic forecasting problems \[[6](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_6)\].

GNNs capture spatial dependency and temporal dependency (road condition changes) for traffic forecasting problems. Let us represent a road network as a graph. Each road segment and sensor for the road is taken as a node and road connection is considered as edge. Best results are produced by GNNs in traffic forecasting problems \[[6](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_6)\].

When a person is planning for the tour, route selection and traveling time estimation are predicted by using GNNs. Companies like Google uses GNNs for traffic forecasting, route planning and traveling time estimation.

The road image is collected through cameras with many objects like vehicles, human, shop, animals, etc. This road image is passed to GNNs model to describe the relationship between the objects. Suppose two vehicles are close to each other we calculate the distance between them and pass the traffic flow forecasting to drivers to avoid accidents. We can also group the similar type of objects and estimate the total vehicles of the same type.

The traffic information is processed to divide the road networks into Super-segments. The Super-segments consists of a set of road segments. Each road segments have specific length and speed. Each neural network model is trained for each Super-segments vary in the length. So, we need separate trained neural network model for each super segments to handle the road infra-structure challenge \[[7](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_7)\].

Google map usage of GNN for determining optimal route and travel time is given in [Figure 5.12](#ch5.xhtml#fig5_12) \[[7](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_7)\].

![Figure 5.12  Google map usage of graph neural network.](images/fig5_12_B.jpg)

[Figure 5.12  Google map usage of graph neural network.](#ch5.xhtml#R_fig5_12)

 

### 5.10.1 Trajectory Forecasting

In order to predict the trajectory of moving objects based on the past trajectory. Trajectory forecasting is vital in self-driving vehicles to take the decision. Moving agent’s trajectories can be forecasted by GNN \[[15](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_15)\].

### 5.10.2 Recurrent Neural Networks (Rnns) For Driving Style Analysis And Vehicle Demand Forecasting

The RNN is the type of deep neural network that is adapted for time series problems of variable length, permitting the use of past information to predict the future information.

#### 5.10.2.1 Driving Style Analysis

The RNN is used to analyze the driving style and GPS error like a vehicle in one place without any movements, internal sensor error and time gap based on the GPS data \[[9](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_9)\].

First, RNN model is trained using the preprocessed past GPS data (Accelerations and Speed). Based on the previous layer output, safety driving style evaluation is learned by the model. Then trained data is used to predict the future acceleration, speed factor and GPS error data for safety driving style. From the RNN driving style evaluation, we can instruct the drivers to improve their driving style for best performance and efficient personal driving \[[9](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_9)\].

The following parameters are considered for safe driving style:

1.  Defensive driving;
2.  Never drive tired;
3.  Avoid distractions;
4.  Stay alert;
5.  Look ahead down the road;
6.  Maintain optimum speed limit;
7.  Wear Seatbelt;
8.  Know your blind spots.

Different people will have different driving styles. So, the risk level varies for different driving styles. Identify and rate the drivers depending on their driving style.

### 5.10.3 Vehicle Demand Forecasting

Bidirectional gated RNNs are used for vehicle forecasting on demand. Each and every time, destination and departure demand on all stations are calculated. Both destination and departure values are summed and given to the activation function to calculate the predicted output. The model is learnt from current previous data (past hourly data).

## 5.11 Applications of RNN

Traffic speed, congestion flow and forecasting are predicted with the LSTM and gated RNNs.

### 5.11.1 Multilayer Perceptron in Transportation

1.  **Traffic Flow Prediction:** Triple layered multilayer perceptron is used to estimate the traffic volume and available parking place. These two factors play an important role in the smart city transportation system.
2.  **Traffic Sign Recognition:** These systems play an important role in reducing the transportation risks. Traffic sign detection and detected sign recognition are the ideal steps for video traffic sequence.
3.  **Multi-Layer Perceptron:** It is used for traffic sign recognition. Vehicle flow will not be the same in all places and time. So, training the multi-layer perceptron with numerous datasets is essential. If the received output is not the same as the predicted output, apply the back propagation algorithm to learn from the past. Multi-layer perceptron model reduces error rate and gives accurate prediction even in the different traffic signs and lighting conditions.

### 5.11.2 Vehicle Classification

The multilayer perceptron is used to classify the Vehicle. The first step in vehicle classification is detecting the vehicle through live camera capture, next extracting the feature from segmentation and the final step is classifying the vehicle based on pattern recognition (invariant features).

Viola-Jones algorithm is used for detecting the vehicle. The components of Viola-Jones object Detection algorithm are cascaded classifier, AdaBoost, Haar-Like feature and integral image. Haar-Like is a rectangular feature used as an input for cascaded classifier. In AdaBoost each cycle of boosting a feature among all other potential features is selected and, in the end, the final classification will be a linear combination of the initial weak classification. Integral image is a quick method for calculating the Haar-Like feature. For improving the processing speed and fast rejection of error windows, Cascaded classifier is used \[[10](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_10)\].

## 5.12 Autoencoders and Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM) in Transportation

### 5.12.1 Demand Prediction and Rare Events

The transportation should be increased to the place where the demand for the vehicle is high. Convolutional-LSTM is used for demand prediction based on spatial-temporal features taken from different input. Accident report is prepared after it happened. But if we can avoid the accidents in future by predicting accident features by autoencoder and traffic flow is modeled using long, short term memory (LSTM) \[[13](#ch5.xhtml#ref5_13)\].

## 5.13 Summary

The neural network works like the human brain. The artificial neuron can communicate with other neurons and also learn from the past data to improve the accuracy in prediction and decision making. The transportation influences the country economy. The neural networks play the vital role to make the transportation as intelligent transportation system. In this chapter, neural networks computing capabilities and how neural networks make the intelligence in different transport applications is explored. The problems in the transportation are solved by using different neural networks.

Below neural network approaches can be used for various transportation applications like traffic forecasting, route planning, vehicle number plate detection, parking space identification, vehicle density, travel time, autonomous vehicle, vehicle location, vehicle speed, driver behavior, traffic management, etc.

1.  **Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs):** These are used in transportation applications like traffic characteristics and traffic incident risks prediction, vehicle identification, traffic signal timing, passenger demand-based transportation, AVs, traffic signal lights are changed based on the traffic, license plate recognition.
2.  **Graph Neural Network (GNN):** These are applied to forecast the traffic and trajectory.
3.  **Recurrent Neural Networks (RNNs):** These are used for driving style analysis and vehicle demand forecasting.
4.  **Multilayer Perceptron:** It is used for traffic flow prediction, traffic sign recognition, vehicle classification.
5.  **Demand Prediction and Rare Events:** These are done by autoencoders and LSTM.

# Chapter 6 Statistical AI Model in an Intelligent Transportation System

A. KODIESWARI^([⁴](#fn4))

## Abstract

The usage of vehicles has been increased over the past two decades due to increase in world population which leads traffic jamming, travel delays, road accidents and energy consumption. To solve this issue, intelligent transport system needs to be developed using powerful predictive model.

This chapter focuses on smart statistical AI models to address the issues of transport infrastructure development, traffic forecasting and driver behavior detection that avoids traffic congestion and accident. Neural network and artificial intelligence (AI) models provide an impact in the design of intelligent transportation model. The artificial neural networks (ANNs) are used to extract the patterns to develop transportation infrastructure systems (TIS). GNN ensures the estimated time of arrival by analyzing the traffic congestion to overcome traffic jams. Deep Convolution Neural Network is used to detect the behavior of driver based on the characteristics like acceleration, gravity, throttle, and revs per minute (RPM) to reduce accidental risks. All these AI and neural network models bring safety to the civic, cost-effective, and reliable transportation system.

## 6.1  Introduction to Neural Networks

When building a neural network, the node layers are organized in a manner that is comparable to how neurons in the human brain interact with one another. Additionally, it is referred to as stimulated neural networks (SNN) or artificial neural networks (ANNs), and it is a critical component of machine learning (ML) applications. The node layers of ANNs comprises of three layers:

1.  **Input Layer:** The very first layer of the neural network is the input layer. The data which needs to be processed will be given to the input layer. The input layer consists of the input nodes which will proceed the data to the subsequent nodes for further processing of the network.
2.  **Hidden Layer:** There is a hidden layer in neural networks that sits between the algorithm’s input and output, assigning weights to the inputs and using an activation function as an output to steer the network in the desired direction. To summarize, the hidden layers alter the inputs to the network in a nonlinear manner. The number of hidden layers has an effect on the performance of the neural network, and the weights assigned to those layers may have an effect on its performance. The hidden layers permit splitting down the functionality of the neural network into particular information transformations. Each function in the hidden layer is optimized to accomplish a certain result. Consider a neural network which needs to perform face recognition. The role of the hidden layer in this particular network is to split the process of face recognition to make it more effective. In order to identify the face using the network. It may split the process like one hidden layer may predict the eyes another may predict the nose on their own the network will independently recognize the objects, and they can operate together in a neural network to predict the whole object like face.
3.  **Output Layer:** It may consist of the output nodes. The primary responsibility of this layer is to perform computations and transmitting information from the network to the outside world. The output from this layer only will be given to the user. The information from this layer will be in the form which could be understandable by the user.

## 6.2  Working of Neural Networks

Widely recognized as neural networks, ANNs and neural networks are two words that refer to the same idea. Using nodes, an artificial neural network may be divided into layers, such as the input layer, the hidden layer, and the output layer, which are then linked together to form the network. Each artificial neuron in the network that is linked to another neuron in the network is assigned a weight and a threshold. Whenever the output of a node reaches a certain threshold, the network deems the node to be active and transfers data to the next network layer. When the output node is less than the threshold value, then the data will not be permitted to move to the next layer. The prediction, classification, and clustering algorithms can be performed in the neural network by training the model with the available data set for a certain task. So, from the training data set, the neural network will learn, and this may help to increase the performance and accuracy of the network. Training data is a process used by neural networks to learn in order to increase their accuracy over time during the prediction. When these learning algorithms are tuned for accuracy, however, they become extremely powerful tools in the computer science and artificial intelligence (AI) domain, allowing anybody to easily categorize and organize data. When compared to manual categorization by human specialists, speech recognition and picture recognition operations can be done in minutes rather than hours. Google’s search results are created using one of the most well-known neural networks in the world of networks ([Figure 6.1](#ch6.xhtml#fig6_1)).

![Figure 6.1 Working of neural networks.](images/fig6_1_B.jpg)

[Figure 6.1 Working of neural networks.](#ch6.xhtml#R_fig6_1)

Each and every node of a neural network will have its own input data, weights, a bias and an output. This can be estimated using the formula:

  
$$\\begin{array}{l} {\\sum\_{i = l}^{m}{wixi + bias = w\_{1}x\_{1} + w\_{2}x\_{2} + w\_{3}x\_{3} + bias}} \\\\ {Output = f\\left( x \\right) = \\left\\{ \\begin{array}{l} {1if{\\sum{w1x1 + b \\geq 0}}} \\\\ {0if{\\sum{w1x1 + b < 0}}} \\\\ \\end{array} \\right.} \\\\ \\end{array}$$  

The process carried out in each node of the neural network is discussed below:

*   Once the input layer receives the input data, the weights will be assigned to it.
*   Input data will be multiplied with the assigned weights and then summed with a bias value.
*   The obtained output of the node is supposed to pass through the activation function, and which will determine whether the output is capable of passing through or not.
*   The initial threshold value will be fixed in the activation function. If the obtained output value exceeds the threshold, then the data of the node will fire, and if the obtained value is below the threshold value, then the data of the node will not fire.
*   So, the output obtained from one node will be given as input for the node in the next layer.
*   The neural network will be built by passing the data from one node to the next node.

Let us apply this in a real time example – Sheela wants to buy a dress. She is supposed to take a decision which may be either Yes ([1](#ch6.xhtml#ref6_1)) or No (0). To make the decision, Sheela needs to focus on a few influencing factors of the decision making. The factors are:

*   Whether the dress fits her perfectly?
*   Is the material of the dress good?
*   Does the color suit her?

The network will check for these conditions before making a decision. Let us assume these as inputs. So, now we have three input parameters, and the input are:

*   X[1] = 1, Because the dress fits her perfectly.
*   X[2] = 0, Material of the dress is good.
*   X[3] = 1, Color suits nicely.

Then, the weight for these inputs will be assigned according to the influence of that particular factor on the decision-making:

*    W1–5, Since fitting is the major factor, it holds high weightage.
*    W2–2, Material is the least factor considered by Sheela.
*    W3–3, Color of the dress.

Where the weight is decided according to the priorities that Sheela prefers. Apply the values in the formula:

  
$$\\begin{array}{l} {Y = \\left( {1 \\times 5} \\right) + \\left( {0 \\times 2} \\right) + \\left( {1 \\times 3} \\right) - 3} \\\\ {Y = 8 - 3} \\\\ {Y = 6} \\\\ \\end{array}$$  

So, the obtained output value is greater than the threshold value (which is 1). It indicates that the neuron can fire to the next layer now. If the value has less means, then the neuron is allowed to fire to the next layer.

In order to build a neural network, the following building blocks are required for handling of artificial neural network:

*   Network topology;
*   Adjustments of weights or learning; • Activation functions.

### 6.2.1 Network Topology

The organization of a network’s nodes and interconnecting links is referred to as its topology. ANN may be further can be categorized as follows based on their topology:

*   feedforward neural network; and
*   feedback neural network.

### 6.2.2 Feedforward Network

It’s a non-recurrent network with layers of processing units/nodes, all of which can be connected to the nodes of the earlier levels. Different weights are positioned on the connection. The signal can only go in one direction, from input to output, because there is no feedback loop. It may be classified into two categories ([Figure 6.2](#ch6.xhtml#fig6_2)).

![Figure 6.2 Types of feed forward network.](images/fig6_2_B.jpg)

[Figure 6.2 Types of feed forward network.](#ch6.xhtml#R_fig6_2)

 

### 6.2.3 Single Layer Feedforward Network

Single layer feed-forward network is the simple type of neural network which can establish a connection to the next layer neuron only. In this network, the cyclic connection between the nodes is not possible. Here, in this network data can be transmitted only in the forward direction. In a single layer feed forward network, there will be an input and output layers alone in the network. It will not have any hidden layers in between these layers ([Figure 6.3](#ch6.xhtml#fig6_3)).

![Figure 6.3 Architecture of single layer feed forward network.](images/fig6_3_B.jpg)

[Figure 6.3 Architecture of single layer feed forward network.](#ch6.xhtml#R_fig6_3)

### 6.2.4 Multilayer Feedforward Network

Multilayer neural networks can be employed mainly on classificationbased problems for non-linear types of data sets. In a multilayer neural network, the hidden layer will be included in between the input and the output layers of the network. The output of the input layer will be given as input for the hidden layer then the data will be processed to the output layer. There are no feedback connections, where the output of the network will be given as given again for the next iteration ([Figure 6.4](#ch6.xhtml#fig6_4)).

![Figure 6.4 Architecture of multi-layer neural network.](images/fig6_4_B.jpg)

[Figure 6.4 Architecture of multi-layer neural network.](#ch6.xhtml#R_fig6_4)

### 6.2.5 Feedback Neural Network

In a feedback neural network, the data can be transmitted in both the forward as well as backward direction. The process will be carried out similar to the feed forward neural network like the input data will be multiplied with the assigned weight and summed with the bias. Here, the network will be sending the output as input for the next iteration and the process will be carried out until the network reaches the equilibrium point. So, we can usually consider the feedback neural network in a dynamic state. These networks can be used in addressable memories ([Figure 6.5](#ch6.xhtml#fig6_5)).

![Figure 6.5 Architecture of back propagation neural networks.](images/fig6_5_B.jpg)

[Figure 6.5 Architecture of back propagation neural networks.](#ch6.xhtml#R_fig6_5)

 

A feedback neural network also called a recurrent neural network (RNN) can be a powerful and robust type of network used in sequential type of data or time series type of data. RNNs is an extended model of feed forward neural network. Generally, the RNN will recognize sequential characteristics of data and then use the patterns to predict the next likelihood using the recognized pattern. It is widely used in cases where prediction is difficult. The processed output data will be given as input for the previous state using a feedback loop. The feedback loop makes it persist and often described as memory.

### 6.2.6 Adjustment of Weights

The weights in neural networks prove the strength of connection among two different neurons. It influences the system with appropriate input parameters. These weights are assigned to each node in the network. Bias are constant values that best fit the model with given input data. Once the model is trained with data, it should be further tested for accuracy. The error in the model is the difference between the actual and predicted values. Based on these errors, the system performs weight adjustments and propagates them backwards to the network. This process is called back propagation of error.

### 6.2.7 Activation Function

The activation function determines whether or not a neuron should be stimulated by calculating a weighted sum and then adding bias to it. Non-linearity is introduced into a neuron’s output by using an activation function. Based on the output inaccuracy, we’d tweak the neurons’ weights and biases in a neural network. This process is referred to as back-propagation. Activation functions enable back-propagation since the gradients and error are provided at the same time for updating the weights and biases. A neural network is nothing more than a linear regression model without an activation function. A non-linear activation function changes the input so that it can learn and do more difficult tasks. There are five important activation functions. They are:

*   Linear activation function;
*   Sigmoid activation function;
*   Tanh activation function;
*   ReLu activation function;
*   SoftMax activation function.

#### 6.2 .7.1 Linear Activation Function

This function is represented by the equation y = mx + c, it is similar to the equation of a straight line. The linear activation function f(x) can be represented as:

  
*f*(*x*) = *x*  

The ultimate activation function of the last layer is nothing more than a linear function of the input to the first layer if all layers are linear in nature.

#### 6.2.7.2 Sigmoid Activation Function

This function receives any real value as input and provides any value between 0 and 1 as outcome. The sigmoid activation function is shown below:

  
$$f\\left( x \\right) = \\frac{1}{1 + e^{- x}}$$  

The closer the output value is to 1.0, the larger the value of the input (more positive), and the closer the output value is to 0.0, the bigger the value of the input (more negative). Models where the output is a probability prediction commonly employ this. This is due to the fact that the probability is somewhere between 0 and 1. This function is suitable in the range 3 to –3, after which the graph becomes smoothened to zero \[[15](#ch6.xhtml#ref6_15)\].

#### 6.2.7.3 Tanh Activation Function

This function is nearly identical to sigmoid/logistic activation in that it has a comparable output range of –1 to 1, and even has the same S-shape. Tanh Function is represented by:

  
$$f\\left( x \\right) = \\frac{\\left( {e^{x} - e^{- x}} \\right)}{\\left( {e^{x} - e^{- x}} \\right)}$$  

The output will be closer to 1.0 if the input is greater (more positive), and the output will be closer to –1.0 if the input is smaller (more negative). Because the tanh activation function’s output is Zero-centered, we may easily translate the output values as highly negative, neutral, or very positive.

#### 6.2 .7.4 Relu Activation Function

ReLu is an abbreviation for Rectified Linear Unit. It is accomplished as a derivative function rather than a linear function. It is represented by:

  
*f*(*x*) = max (0,*x*)  

It allows a feedback network model to convert the system into an efficient model. The ReLU function does not simultaneously stimulate all of the neurons. These neurons are turned inactive, when the output of the transfer function is less than 0. It is more efficient than sigmoid and tanh activation functions. Leaky ReLu is an improved version of the ReLu function. It provides the backpropagation for positive as well as negative values.

#### 6.2.7.5 Softmax Activation Function

The SoftMax activation function calculates the relative probabilities. The function is represented as:

  
$$soft\\max\\left( z\_{i} \\right) = \\frac{\\exp\\left( z\_{i} \\right)}{\\sum\_{j}{\\exp\\left( z\_{i} \\right)}}$$  

The function is appropriate for multi-class classification. It returns the largest value in the list of values. The SoftMax function returns a probability of class membership for each class label and tries to approximate the predicted target for a given input as closely as possible.

## 6.3  Mathematical Models For Transportation Research

Transportation research techniques were developed in the early 1960s. These methods were invented with a minimal number of samples or data. In this modern era of computing, large amounts of samples can be collected, stored, and retrieved. The main challenge would be to deal with the nonlinearity that can be seen in the data. Most of the models are linear statistical models.

Now the transportation research field is moving towards a drastic development in terms of efficiency. The data which is abundant in the current modern era can be used to identify patterns in them. It is also capable of dealing with noise and even uncertainties. One of the most important objectives is to collect large volumes of data in the transportation domain. Communication systems and sensor technology plays a vital role in data extraction from the real world. The efficacy of these technologies helps in improving the system to identify appropriate patterns in the data. Neural networks pave the way for finding various hotspots in transportation research.

Computational neural network replicates the functionalities of human intelligence. There are plenty of Computational Neural Network models available to solve the real-world issues in the transportation domain. Let us consider the feedforward neural network to express it with a clear mathematical model. Various technologies emerged in recent times helps in improving the computation efficiency of the systems. Research on computational, physics, cognitive science, statistics contributes to the development of neural models in depicting human intelligence. These intelligent systems are capable of processing information that the human brain processes.

A neural network is made up of nodes that are interconnected and work similar to neurons in the human brain. The deep learning algorithms can be applied to recognize the pattern in given data, classify the data, cluster them and so on. Neural networks are a computational model which holds a network architecture made up of artificial neurons. They have applications in various areas like search engines, recommendation systems, smart personal assistants and much more.

Neural networks have its hindrance in domains like e-commerce, Logistics, Healthcare, Finance, Security. It is widely used for personalizing the user’s experience in shopping, predicting the best route for travel, examining patient’s health, monitoring the patients’ health status periodically, detecting fraudulent actions, forecasting the stock market growth, and protecting from system attacks. Hence, Neural networks are used in almost every place wherever intelligence is adopted.

The main objective of neural networks is to transform the input into significant output. The network is made of artificial neurons which take multiple inputs and process them to generate a worthwhile outcome from the system. Each neuron has an influence on the other neurons. It is capable of observing the data with complex patterns. Neural networks have many layers. Individually each layer proposes to perform a specific function. Hence, the model of a neuron in a neural network is termed to be a perceptron. The perceptron takes several inputs and processes it to produce a single binary output. A neuron typically consists of input values, weights, transfer function, activation function, and output value. The weights are real numbers that depict the importance of the input feature. [Figure 6.6](#ch6.xhtml#fig6_6) shows the structure of a neuron.

![Figure 6.6 Structure of a neuron depicting the human brain.](images/fig6_6_B.jpg)

[Figure 6.6 Structure of a neuron depicting the human brain.](#ch6.xhtml#R_fig6_6)

In the given figure, input values (X[i]) indicate the input feature values that are fed to the model to train the model for better accuracy. These features are to be chosen appropriately, such that they are closely related to the task to be accomplished. The most important feature is provided with more weights (W[i]) to increase their contribution to the learning process. Certain features create a great impact in deciding the outcome of the task. For instance, the road condition creates a great impact in the travel timing between given spots for any traveler. A threshold is set for the entire process. The output is determined by the weighted sum of the dot product of all the input features and their corresponding weights. The target output is 0, whenever the weighted sum is less than the chosen threshold value. Also, The target output is 1, whenever the weighted sum is greater than the given threshold value.

  
$$Output,Y = \\left\\{ \\begin{matrix} {0,f{\\sum{X\_{i}W\_{i} < threshold}}} \\\\ {1,{\\sum{X\_{i}W\_{i} > threshold}}} \\\\ \\end{matrix} \\right.$$  

## 6.4  Algorithmic Neural Network Model for Transportation Research

In a neural network, node layers are arranged in a manner similar to how neurons in the human brain communicate with one another. Neural Networks is also known as Artificial Neural Network (ANN) and Stimulated Neural Network (SNN) and it is said to be subset of Machine Learning and Deep Learning algorithm. There are three types of node layers in ANNs: the input layer, a hidden layer, and an output layer.

The weights and thresholds of each artificial neuron linked to another artificial neuron are the same. An individual node is engaged when its output exceeds a predetermined threshold value, and data is sent to the next tier of the network. When the output node is less than the threshold value, no data is sent. Classification, clustering, function approximation, time-series analysis, and forecasting problems like these are all solved with neural network.

*   Traffic forecasting and control;
*   Evaluation of traffic parameters;
*   Maintenance of transport infrastructure;
*   Transport policy;
*   Driver behavior and autonomous vehicles (AVs).

Best neural network techniques to meet the transportation research challenges are discussed as in subsections.

### 6.4.1 Artificial Neural Networks (ANNS) for Transportation Infrastructure Systems (TIS)

With their exceptional capability to infer referring from complex or erroneous data, ANNs are used to extract complex patterns from data and recognize drifts that are too complex to be detected by people or some other intelligence-based approaches. The concept of ANN was presented by Zhang & Zhang (1999), and it was created to imitate the functioning and structure of neural systems in living beings. ANNs is considered to be a powerful tool for various applications like medicine, transportation, optimization, quantum physics, text classification, language generation, speech recognition and so on.

ANNs are made up of a huge number of neurons, which are connected in parallel and work together to solve various issues.

Input and target data are used to train the ANN. ANNs also illustrate the whole interconnections of the models along with the numeric weights of the nodes that may be changed depending on experience, allowing neural nets to adapt to inputs and learn. Thus, ANN is considered to be a powerful and flexible tool for seizing non-linear and complex physical process with a high degree of accuracy.

#### 6.4.1.1 Advantages of Artificial Neural Network

*   Works with large set of data amount of data set;
*   Detects complex nonlinear interactions between dependent and independent variables implicitly;
*   Indicate each and every potential connection amongst the predictor variables;
*   Also solve complex problems in TIS (transportation infrastructure systems).

#### 6.4.1.2  Design of Transportation Infrastructure Systems (Tis)

Transportation infrastructures are defined as the interconnection of physical and perceptible assets necessary to sustenance and develop a country. It is consequently critical to effectively manage the Transportation Infrastructure in order to deliver continuous, long-term, and cost-effective amenities to the population. The design of TIS is a critical component of effective administration.

Many competing variables are currently affecting the Transportation sector, necessitating rigorous system considerations like high design and construction capital costs, legislative changes, and the influences to increased requirement and utilization of Transportation Infrastructure. Planning land usage, building construction, and land subdivision are all parts of transportation infrastructure planning and development that are supervised by the governments at all levels. The goal is to produce highquality and long-term development outcomes in both urban and rural areas, and also accounting crucial problems like environmental preservation as part of a larger Transportation Infrastructure Planning and Development analysis. For effective development of TIS, the system engineering methodologies are highly required.

Transport infrastructure engineering requirements consists of four core elements, and they are ([Figure 6.7](#ch6.xhtml#fig6_7)):

![Figure 6.7 TIS parameters.](images/fig6_7_B.jpg)

[Figure 6.7 TIS parameters.](#ch6.xhtml#R_fig6_7)

 

*   Infrastructure asset rehabilitation and renewal;
*   Structural performance and operation;
*   Sustainable and tangible materials;
*   Emerging robust technologies and innovative tools.

The general system engineering methods involves:

  
*T**X*[*n*] = ∑*W**Q*[*n*] = *O**X*[*n**q*]  

where; TX[n] is the transportation system parameters input; *W* is the TIMS weights; *Q*[n] is the function; and *OX*[nq] is the output of TIMS functions.

#### 6.4.1.3 Transportation Infrastructure Management System (Tims)

The straightforward approach to improve the performance of transport infrastructure systems (TIS) is transportation infrastructure management system (TIMS). TIMS is a highly specialized method that works with a wide range of transportation characteristics. TIMS consists of two subsystems, and they are pavement management system (PMS) and bridge management system (BMS). The objective of TIMS is to collection of various sizable data related to transportation infrastructure. To improve the performance of TIMS, a conditional enhancement method is used. Infrastructure damage detection and monitoring is another challenging task for TIMS since the visual evaluation strategy is used traditionally, which consumes more time and is difficult in structure diagnosis of the state. This affects the performance of TIMS. To overcome, an ANN approach is used which involves structural parameter reduction for damage detection and diagnosis of constituent material state for monitoring process.

#### 6.4.1.4 Architectural Ann Design of Transport Infrastructure Systems

When it comes to data mining prediction and classification, ANNs are a data-driven tool ([Figure 6.8](#ch6.xhtml#fig6_8)).

![Figure 6.8 Artificial neural network function or transportation infrastructure system.](images/fig6_8_B.jpg)

[Figure 6.8 Artificial neural network function or transportation infrastructure system.](#ch6.xhtml#R_fig6_8)

Furthermore, ANNs provide unique tools and applications like Control devices that, whenever implemented appropriately, may provide substantial advantages in sectors like Transportation Infrastructure. The suggested model is a dependable prototype with additional benefits, especially in terms of Process Control and System Performance predictions. Because process control and system performance are regarded essential components of TIS, TIS, and TIMS must be included in any artificial Neural Network collaboration. TIS ANN is a multilayer general system engineering solution. Method includes input, processing, and output phases. This model’s overall performance must be consistently improved and maintained in order to aid in the overall development of priorities. Furthermore, all three main system phases must be properly studied and integrated holistically when evaluating system performance enhancement.

### 6.4.2 Traffic Forecasting Using Graph Neural Network (Gnn)

Traffic prediction and estimated times of arrival (ETA) are the important factors to overcome traffic jams and running to late hours. Traffic forecasting helps friends or family members to intimate the late arrival and also the service providers to pass the change in pick-up or drop timing information. Google Map computes the ETA by analyzing the traffic data from road segments using ML algorithms. The ML algorithm analysis the future traffic by comparing the live traffic data with the historic road traffic patterns. But it is complex to compute the exact traffic in roads due to inevitable variations in rush hours. Also, other added challenges for predictive models are road quality, speed limits, accidents, and closures.

#### 6.4.2.1 Working Principle of Graph Neural Networks (GNNS) in Google Maps

The working principle of the existing ML model for traffic forecasting resides on super segments, but estimating the travel times with super segments are found to be difficult. Multiple neighboring segments of road that share significant traffic volume are referred to as “super segments.” Super segment covers to a group of road segments, each of which has its own length and speed characteristics. To achieve a high percentage of accuracy, GNNs, a ML model was utilized in recent studies. GNNs, which incorporate relational learning biases for spatiotemporal reasoning, to simulate the connection structure of real-world road networks. GNN will design a traffic model as how cars flow through a network of roads ([Figures 6.9](#ch6.xhtml#fig6_9), [6.10](#ch6.xhtml#fig6_10) and [6.11](#ch6.xhtml#fig6_11)).

The super segment covers a set of road segments in which each segment has particular length and speed features. Initially the RNN was used to handle variable length sequences of super segments and found difficulty in forming the structure from the road network. The RNN is deployed by the concept of “proximity” and allows complex connectivity to handle traffic ahead, behind us, adjacent, and intersection roads. GNN is recommended for traffic modeling, and it is an extended learning inclination of CNN (convolution neural network) ([Figure 6.12](#ch6.xhtml#fig6_12)).

![Figure 6.12 Working of deep neural networks.](images/fig6_12_B.jpg)

[Figure 6.12 Working of deep neural networks.](#ch6.xhtml#R_fig6_12)

![Figure 6.9 The model architecture for determining optimal routes and their travel time.](images/fig6_9_B.jpg)

[Figure 6.9 The model architecture for determining optimal routes and their travel time.](#ch6.xhtml#R_fig6_9)

![Figure 6.10 Working principle of advanced graph neural networks in google maps.](images/fig6_10_C.jpg)

[Figure 6.10 Working principle of advanced graph neural networks in google maps.](#ch6.xhtml#R_fig6_10)

In a GNN, the local road network is treated as a graph. It also includes the node for each route segment and consecutive edges of the same road or associated through a crossroads. In our model, the states of nodes and result of edges are determined by neural network when a message passing algorithm is executed. So, it is understood that super segments are considered as road networks sub graphs, which were sampled at random in proportion to traffic density. These sampled sub graphs are trained using single models and deployed at a system. In GNN model the adjacent nodes send messages to one another and by appointing local bias the nodes will find confidential adjacent nodes with one message passing step. This system enables the GNN to develop the connectivity structure for the effective road networks. The adjacent roads not connected with the main road also predicted accurately which makes GNN more powerful experimentally. Nonetheless single route or long route, GNN performs the same process according to varying length and complexity.

![Figure 6.11 Functions of recurrent neural network.](images/fig6_11_B.jpg)

[Figure 6.11 Functions of recurrent neural network.](#ch6.xhtml#R_fig6_11)

 

#### 6.4.2.2 Build Generalize Model

Since there are so many variations in the graph topologies utilized during training, when the model is put into production, it becomes extremely sensitive to changes in the training curriculum. A single batch can include anything from a two-node tiny network to a huge graph with more than 100 nodes \[[11](#ch6.xhtml#ref6_11)\]. The constraints of GNN were solved by utilizing a revolutionary reinforcement learning (RL) technique known as supervised learning. When a ML model learns anything new, it forgets essential features it has already learnt. As a result, the learning rate of the model is reduced over time by the researchers. After a predetermined time of training, the parameters are normalized using an exponentially declining learning rate schedule.

To overcome the error in learning rate during training runs, MetaGradients RL algorithm is used to obtain optimal learning rate dynamically during training runs. The Meta Gradients readjust the learning rates during training runs, enable to reduce the learning rate and further improve the quality at a greater rate automatically. Hence this approach is most widely used in production as it induces more stable result.

Generic models with great potential to reduce travel estimate errors are built using a linear combination of multiple loss functions. We used a regularizing factor on the model weights, L2 and L1 losses on the overall traversal times, and individual Huber and negative-log likely (NLL) losses for each node in the graph to produce a multi-loss target \[[12](#ch6.xhtml#ref6_12)\]. We avoided over-fitting on the training dataset by including these losses into our model. Training advancements were more quickly transmitted to held-out test sets and end-to-end trials even though our judgments of training quality were unchanged.

## 6.5 Deep Convolutional Neural Networks (Cnns) for Driver Behavior Detection

Convolutional neural networks (CNNs) is an ordinary approach that uses deep learning techniques and neural network architectures with more than one hidden layer. Deep Neural Network is a model with multiple hidden layers. CNNs are also known as deep neural networks. Transportation is a primary mode of economic impact for any developing countries and recently there is drastic increase in the number of vehicles on road. Due to increase in vehicles the road safety is a severe problem around the world. Hence to reduce accidental risk it is responsible to detect the driver behavior using ITS (intelligent transportation systems). The traditional computer vision techniques suffered with spoofing and privacy violation issues. By utilizing deep learning algorithms, it is possible to examine driver behavior using a variety of characteristics, including as acceleration, gravity, throttle, and revolutions per minute (RPM). When using the criteria, the approach can determine if a driver is being safe, aggressive or distracted while driving. It may also determine whether the driver is intoxicated while driving. The value of deep neural networks on pictures may be utilized by using CNNs on images to build models from driving signals using recurrence plot (RP) approaches ([Figure 6.13](#ch6.xhtml#fig6_13)).

![Figure 6.13 Process of driver behavior detection.](images/fig6_13_B.jpg)

[Figure 6.13 Process of driver behavior detection.](#ch6.xhtml#R_fig6_13)

The deep CNN techniques for driver behavior detection involve:

*   Data acquisition;
*   Implementing time window and recurrence plot technique;
*   Convolution neural networks.

Time window is applied on 9 collected signals to differentiate the driver behavior. The window data are then converted into image using RP technique. The converted image enables to convert temporal dependencies into spatial dependency. The images that are generated will be given as input to CNNs to classify different driving styles such as normal, aggressive, distracted, drowsy, and drunk driving. The efficient and low-cost computational model will be designed based on number of convolution layers, number of filters and filter size ([Figure 6.14](#ch6.xhtml#fig6_14)).

![Figure 6.14 Different driving style from SEU dataset.](images/fig6_14_B.jpg)

[Figure 6.14 Different driving style from SEU dataset.](#ch6.xhtml#R_fig6_14)

### 6.5.1 Data Acquisition

We require the vehicle’s linear acceleration regardless of the vehicle’s orientation to examine its movement. The linear acceleration is a measurement of the pressure applied to a vehicle along all three axes (x, y, and z), except the force of gravity. To compute linear acceleration, we need both acceleration and gravity inputs in all three directions (x, y, and z). We consider acceleration and gravity to be two properties that the deep neural network is tasked with evaluating and learning representations for.

Revolutions per minute is a unit of measurement for the number of times an engine rotates around a fixed axis in one minute. ECU parameters must be accessed via an OBDII adapter in order to acquire an accurate RPM value. According to how firmly the accelerator pedal is pressed, the throttle position sensor decides how far the throttle valve will open up. The OBDII adaptor retrieves this value from the ECU. RPM changes in proportion to throttle in most, but not all, conditions, such as uphill or downhill routes. This is especially true in manual (non-automated) cars. As a result, while making predictions about driving behavior, these features are taken into account.

A driver was simulated who engaged in aggressive behaviors such as speeding, tailgating, excessive acceleration, and slowing down when taking off or braking. A fake activity was used to draw the driver’s attention away from the road for 1 to 2 seconds, simulating distracted driving.

To mimic sleepy driving, participants closed their eyes for a random period between 1 and 2 seconds, seldom applied the brake, had sluggish steering, drove slower than the speed limit, and had minimal change in acceleration or gear. A sleepy driver, in reality, will ultimately wake up and either correct the vehicle’s position or cause an accident.

### 6.5.2 Implementing Time Window and Recurrence Plot (RP) Technique

We record the behavior of drivers in each time slot, which is classified as normal, aggressive, inattentive, drowsy, or inebriated. The 5 s time frame is used for detecting drunk driving, a 20 s time window for detecting aggressive driving and a 1.8 s time window is used for identifying distracted driving. Data is gathered in the time domain and is dependent on time. The recurrence plot (RP) is a time series visualization tool to visualize recurrent tools.

The mathematical model of recurrent plot is:

  
*R*[*i*, *j*]^(*X*) = *θ*(*ϵ*−∥*X*[*i*]−*X*[*j*]∥)  

where; ‖.‖ is the norm; *x* is the time series; *θ* is the Heaviside function; and ϵ is the recurrence threshold.

### 6.5.3 Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNS) Architecture

An engine’s revolutions per minute (RPM) is a unit of measurement. ECU parameters must be retrieved using an OBDII adapter. With a throttle position sensor, you can precisely control how much the throttle valve opens when you depress the accelerator pedal all the way down. The OBDII adaptor collects data from the ECU and sends it to the computer.

However, this is not always the case. For example, the number of RPM fluctuates in relation to throttle in uphill or downhill routes, and especially in manual (non-automated) cars. Because of this, these qualities are taken into account when making predictions about a driver.

CNN architecture is thought to be the most accurate and computationally efficient model for detecting driver behavior. The adoption of a novel CNN architecture eliminates the need of a large number of learnt parameters in a very deep fully connected network. Instead, at the conclusion of CNN, we use a very tiny fully linked network with global max pooling. These new CNN trends demonstrate outstanding efficiency with minimal parameters in numerous applications, and they are also less prone to overfitting. The efficiency and quicker convergence features of the leaky ReLU activation function are used ([Figure 6.15](#ch6.xhtml#fig6_15)).

![Figure 6.15 Convolution neural networks architecture.](images/fig6_15_B.jpg)

[Figure 6.15 Convolution neural networks architecture.](#ch6.xhtml#R_fig6_15)

All input images for CNN’s three channels are 150 × 50 pixels in size. In the initial layers of the CNN, the convolutional and max pooling layers function as feature extractors, at the same time the SoftMax classifier classifies the input pictures into one among five categories in the final layer. Dropout is a normalization technique that reduces overfitting by randomly disregarding certain neurons.

## 6.6  Conclusion

This chapter discussed on AI models to develop Intelligent Transport

System to overcome the confrontation in transport system. The statistical AI models for Transport Infrastructure Management, Traffic forecasting and detection of driver behavior was explained with an application \[[13](#ch6.xhtml#ref6_13)\]. Based on the performance analysis, AI is considered to be an effective model in the design and development of optimal networks for transportation system. Additionally, it helps in the prediction of travel demands, weather-based travel plan, and current traffic state and also diversion management during road accidents. The chapter provides the future scope on CO[2] emission, safe travel, pollution control alert and efficient fuel utilization.
