# Chapter 7 Reinforcement Learning in Smart Transportation

S. MALATHY^([⁵](#fn5))

## Abstract

Artificial intelligence (AI) and machine learning (ML) are currently trending self-learning technologies in the numerous industrial applications. While the majority of existing technology and knowledge are focused on two methodologies as follows: (i) supervised learning; and (ii) unsupervised learning. But the true AI paradigm as presented by nature is the capacity to learn while engaging with the situation.

## 7.1 Supervised Learning

Traditional supervised learning is improbable, because not at all real-world creatures would have been confronted with a set of positive and negative examples. Living beings, on the other hand, learn via interaction and experimentation. They are learning not only a model to differentiate between distinct groups, but also the best policy to achieve the intended result.

Supervised learning is appropriate in the process of learning generalizations to new-fangled situations, but it will not be appropriate in the process of learning via interacting with the environment. The database contains all conceivable interactions, each of which is labeled with the appropriate action. The agent can generalize by inferring a course of action from an unknown environment condition, but it will not find a way of correcting itself, if it is incorrect.

## 7.2 Unsupervised Learning

This technique analyzes and clusters unlabeled datasets using machine learning (ML) techniques. Without the need for human intervention, the algorithms in this technique will uncover data groupings or hidden patterns.

It is the best solution for cross-selling techniques, exploratory data analysis, image identification and consumer segmentation because of its capacity to detect similarities and differences in information.

## 7.3 Reinforcement Learning (RL)

The process of learning tells how to relate conditions to actions which will maximize a reward value numerically is known as RL \[[1](#ch7.xhtml#ref7_1)\]. The system is not taught which actions to take; instead, it must attempt them all to see which ones provide the best results.

RL is a type of semi-supervised learning methodology that falls under the category of policy learning. There are three important factors to be considered in RL as illustrated in [Figure 7.1](#ch7.xhtml#fig7_1):

![Figure 7.1 Factors of reinforcement learning.](images/fig7_1_B.jpg)

[Figure 7.1 Factors of reinforcement learning.](#ch7.xhtml#R_fig7_1)

*   reward;
*   policy; and
*   actions.

### 7.3.1 Policy

A policy describes how an agent acts at a specific point in time. A policy, in general, is a mapping between environmental conditions with the actions and the activities, the agent makes in the environment. In the simplest circumstances, the policy can either be a simple mathematical function or sequence of actions listed in a table, but it can also be a complex computation. Policy is represented as the foundation of the knowledge base (KB) for the agent.

### 7.3.2 Reward

The goal or an objective of a RL problem is defined by a reward. The agent’s actions result in a reward at each time step. The agent’s ultimate goal is to maximize the reward he or she obtains. As a result, the reward distinguishes between the agent’s positive and negative action outcomes. Both pleasure and pain experiences are rewarded.

The primary approach to influence the policy is through the reward; if a policy-selected action results in a lower reward value, then the policy can be altered to select a different deed in the same circumstances.

### 7.3.3 Action or Value Function

A value function describes what is desirable in the long run, whereas the reward signal shows positive activities in an instant sense: each action results in an immediate reward.

The state value is defined as the whole amount of rewards that an agent can obtain from the beginning state. Values represent the enduring attractiveness of a collection of states, taking into consideration the most probable states for the future as well as the sequence of rewards derived from them. In spite of a state provides a modest immediate reward, it might still be valuable since it is frequently followed by states that provide bigger benefits.

### 7.3.4 Importance of Taking Decision on the Values and Rewards

*   Rewards are the primary values which will be obtained immediately after applying an action in RL;
*   Values are secondary projections which follows the obtained rewards.

Each reward is associated with the values. The sole point of calculating values is to obtain additional rewards. Nonetheless, values are considered when making and assessing decisions. Value judgments are ultimately used to guide action decisions. The agent will select the activities that bring the uppermost value states, not the uppermost reward, because those states will initiate the actions that will obtain the most reward in the elongated term.

### 7.3.5 Environment

The environment provides rewards directly, but values are constantly approximated based on tracing the sequences of observations that the agent makes during each and every encounter. As a result, the most significant component of RL algorithms will be the method(s) for effectively estimating values.

### 7.3.6 Model of the Environment

The model of the environment is another crucial component of some RL systems. This is something that replicates environmental behavior and provides assumptions regarding the environment’s behavior. This kind of model will assist an agent in predicting the next reward if a particular action is made, allowing the agent to base current action selection on future environment reactions.

## 7.4 Key Principles in Reinforcement Learning (RL)

[Figure 7.2](#ch7.xhtml#fig7_2) illustrates the key principles of RL.

![Figure 7.2 Key principles of reinforcement learning.](images/fig7_2_B.jpg)

[Figure 7.2 Key principles of reinforcement learning.](#ch7.xhtml#R_fig7_2)

### 7.4.1 Environments and Agents

*   An agent is defined as an object that senses its surroundings with the help of sensors and reacts to them through actuators.
*   Sensors include eyes, ears, and other organs, whereas actuators include legs, wings, and other body parts.
*   A software agent accepts sensory inputs and responds to them by creating files, exhibiting information on the screen and sending packets through the network.
*   At any one time, an agent’s choice of action is determined by what he perceives or has perceived up to that point, but not by what it hasn’t perceived.

### 7.4.2 Agent Function

*   The agent’s behavior is denoted by a function which contains the mapping of an action to the situation.
*   By mapping the agent’s reaction to every sequence, the agent function that translates each sequence to an action is defined.

### 7.4.3 Exploitation Vs. Exploration

In order to increase overall performance, a RL agent would progressively learn the optimal (or near-best) strategy based on brainstorming, considering the reactions of random interactions with the environment. This is possible for an agent by means of two ways as follows:

1.  **Exploration:** Learning better strategies.
2.  **Exploitation:** Exploiting the situation which means greedily using the best strategy available.

Exploration is expensive in terms of resources \[[6](#ch7.xhtml#ref7_6)\], opportunity, and time, a key concern in RL is how to balance exploration into uncharted terrain with the use of current proven tactics. In particular, the agent must balance between what he has learnt thus far by applying greedy exploitation and choosing activities that currently provide the uppermost reward, and uninterruptedly exploring the situation to obtain more information in order to possibly reach a higher long-term value.

*   **Example 1:**

Assume the area to be cleaned is splitted into 9 grids of a square with coordinates G and H, where the values of G = 1, 2, 3 and H = 1, 2, 3 as shown in [Figure 7.3](#ch7.xhtml#fig7_3). The vacuum agent can tell the grid it is present and also whether the grid is dirty or not. It has the option of moving up, down, left or right, sucking up the dirt, or idle, i.e., without doing any action.

[FIGURE 7.3 Grid view of a square for vacuum cleaning problem.](#ch7.xhtml#R_fig7_3)

 
| (1,1) || (1,2) || (1,3) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| (2,1) || (2,2) || (2,3) |
| (3,1) || (3,2) || (3,3) |

*   **Agent Function:** The following is a representation of an agent function:
    
    *   If the present grid of a square is unclean, the agent has to initiate the action of sucking;
    *   Otherwise, it has to move on to the next grid of a square.
*   **Reward:** It’s critical to specify when an agent receives a reward and whether it’s positive or negative. Giving a constructive reward every time the agent scrubs all of the squares is a naive technique. However, because the agent explores at random, there is a slim probability of getting the prize by cleaning all of the squares.
    
    A better technique for guiding the agent in order to achieve the desired goal is to provide a little constructive reward all the time if the vacuum agent scrubs a square and a slight bad reward if the vacuum agent tries to clean a grid of a square that has already been cleaned. When all of the squares have been cleaned, there will be a significant positive reward.
    
*   **Value Function:** The vacuum cleaner problem would be solved by utilizing the value function. Each number represents the most recent approximation of our chances of finishing the cleanup in that state. This estimate is referred to as the state value, and the entire table is referred to as the learned or informed value function.

State A has a greater value is determined as “better” than state B if the present estimate of our chances of winning from the state A is higher than the state B. Because if all of the space has been cleaned, all scenarios in which all of the squares have been cleaned have a probability of 1.

To choose the next move, the agent looks up the current values of the states from each possible motion as follows.

*   **Function 1:** To denote the one of the four directions;
*   **Functions 2 and 3:** Agent scrubs the dust or not.

In most of the attempts, the agent would select the move which results in attaining state possessing the highest value, which means that one with the maximum projected likelihood of identifying an unclean square. Sometimes the agent may choose at random from among the other possible movements. Such moves are referred to as exploratory moves, which allow us to encounter states that will not be encountered otherwise. When we add a reward based on how quickly the space is cleaned to the value function, then the agent would be able to choose a better space traversal approach.

## 7.5 Algorithms for Reinforcement Learning (RL): An Overview

The goal is to present an intuitive overview of known RL methods without delving too deeply into the models or the math behind them. RL is a more sophisticated and difficult way to implement, but it essentially deals with:

*   Learning through collaboration and feedback;
*   Learning to complete a task through trial and error; or
*   Obtaining rewards by applying actions in an environment.

In essence, an agent (or several agents) is created that can sense and understand the circumstances in which it is situated, as well as act and interact with it.

### 7.5.1 Basic Terminologies

The basic terminologies involved in RL \[[3](#ch7.xhtml#ref7_3)\] is shown in [Figure 7.4](#ch7.xhtml#fig7_4):

![Figure 7.4 Basic terminologies of reinforcement learning.](images/fig7_4_B.jpg)

[Figure 7.4 Basic terminologies of reinforcement learning.](#ch7.xhtml#R_fig7_4)

*   The learner and decision-maker are the agent.
*   The agent’s environment is where he or she observes, listens, and then decide what type of actions to be taken next.
*   A collection of actions that the agent can carry out.
*   The agent’s current status in the environment.
*   The environment delivers a reward to an agent for each, and every action performed by it. A scalar value is frequently used.
*   Policy is the agent’s supervisory function (decision-control strategy), which will map the situations to actions.
*   The value of a state is the enduring reward obtained by the beginning state and performing a specific policy.

### 7.5.2 Categories of Reinforcement Learning (RL) Algorithms

*   The challenge of inducing a function from training samples is referred to as the function approximator. Examples for common approximators are as follows:
    
    *   Neural networks;
    *   Nearest-neighbor;
    *   Decision trees algorithms.
*   A probabilistic model:
    
    *   A precise number of states are present in this approach.
    *   The present state and action chosen will be useful for estimating the probability distribution for the future states.
*   Dynamic programming (DP):
    
    *   It is a group of problem-solving techniques for consecutive decision problems with a composite cost structure.
*   Monte Carlo methods:
    
    *   It is a type of value function learning method.
    *   It is used for evaluating the state value by performing many attempts from the beginning state and then averaging all the rewards obtained so far on those attempts.
*   Temporal difference algorithms:
    
    *   It is a type of learning algorithm that compares temporally sequential predictions. Perhaps the most basic concept in all of RL.
*   The agent’s perception of the world is represented by a model that maps state-action pairings to probability estimations over states.

*   **Example 2:**

We have an agent and a prize, but there are a lot of roadblocks along the way. The agent’s job is to find the most efficient route to the reward. The difficulty is better explained in the following problem.

The robot’s objective is to obtain the diamond prize while avoiding the fire obstacles. The robot learns by attempting all feasible courses and then selecting the one that provides the best reward with the fewest obstacles. Each correct step will reward the robot, while each incorrect step will deduct the robot’s pay-out. When you approach the final prize, the diamond, the entire reward will be computed.

The most important aspects of RL are presented in [Figure 7.5](#ch7.xhtml#fig7_5).

![Figure 7.5 Aspects of reinforcement learning.](images/fig7_5_B.jpg)

[Figure 7.5 Aspects of reinforcement learning.](#ch7.xhtml#R_fig7_5)

 

1.  **Input:**
    
    *   The input should be a starting state for the model to work from.
2.  **Training:**
    
    *   Depending on the input, the model will suggest a state; o The user will select whether to give a reward or punishment based on the output and the learning from the previous experience;
    *   The model is constantly evolving;
    *   The optimal solution is determined by the highest possible payment.
3.  **Output:**
    
    *   There are numerous possible outputs, just as there are numerous solutions to a given problem.

The goal of RL is to make judgments in a sequential manner. In simple terms, the output is determined by the current input’s state, and the next input is determined by the previous input’s output. The initial input or the input delivered at the start is decided in supervised learning. Because decisions in RL are reliant on one another, we assign labels to sequences of dependent decisions, whereas in supervised learning, each decision is given its own label.

*   **Benefits of Reinforcement Learning:**
    
    *   Enhances performance;
    *   Change can be sustained for a long time;
    *   Increases behavior;
    *   Disobey the basic standard of performance.
*   **Drawbacks of Reinforcement Learning:**
    
    *   Applying too much of reinforcement might lead to an overabundance of states, lowering the quality of the output;
    *   It only gives enough to meet the bare minimum of requirements.
*   **Applications of Reinforcement Learning:** The various applications of RL \[[5](#ch7.xhtml#ref7_5)\] is given below:
    
    *   Robotics;
    *   Industrial automation;
    *   Data processing;
    *   Constructing training systems.

### 7.5.3 Smart Transportation in Smart Cities

Smart transportation in smart city management system will be designed to handle the cities by incorporating emergency response along with mobility while minimizing traffic congestion on the streets. The integration of sensors, high-speed networks and modern communication technologies with automation are responsible for the progress of smart transportation. The world is entering into new stage of smart transportation called the intelligent or smart transportation systems (STS) and the internet of things (IoT) \[[7](#ch7.xhtml#ref7_7)\].

## 7.6 Intelligent Traffic Management

“Intelligent and smart transportation networks use a variety of technologies for not only managing the transportation systems but also to monitor and evaluate the same \[[2](#ch7.xhtml#ref7_2)\], which in turn will improve the level of safety as well as efficiency” as shown in [Figure 7.6](#ch7.xhtml#fig7_6).

![Figure 7.6 Intelligent traffic management and its parameters.](images/fig7_6_B.jpg)

[Figure 7.6 Intelligent traffic management and its parameters.](#ch7.xhtml#R_fig7_6)

These systems are based on the three parameters stated below:

*   Management;
*   Efficiency;
*   Safety.

To put it another way, smart transportation makes movement about a city in following dimensions:

*   Cost-effective;
*   Safer;
*   More convenient.

### 7.6.1 What New Technology Are Enabling These New Possibilities?

The spread of 5G communication technology with the IoT devices are the significant factors of STS. IoT devices are made of with inexpensive controllers and sensors on a physical machine which is maintained remotely. 5G technology provides the low-latency and the high-speed connectivity needed for smart and intelligent transportation network control and administration.

Smart transportation is already being implemented in a number of places, with the lessons learned from their triumphs and failures being applied to new systems. A few examples are listed below:

*   Major hubs such as New York City have incorporated the innovation of smart transportation as part of their ongoing transformation into a smart city.
*   The rural state of Wyoming, on the other hand, is a prominent testbed for linked automobiles not only increases the efficiency of the supply chain but also minimize the necessity for long distance traveling truckers who must balance the fully loaded schedules with the manpower for the rest.

## 7.7 The Most Important Advantages of Transportation Technology

### 7.7.1 Information from Intelligent Traffic Management Systems

There are various advantages realized in smart city when it is enriched with smart transportation as presented in [Figure 7.7](#ch7.xhtml#fig7_7).

![Figure 7.7 Advantages of smart transportation.](images/fig7_7_B.jpg)

[Figure 7.7 Advantages of smart transportation.](#ch7.xhtml#R_fig7_7)

 

#### 7.7.1.1 Safer

Autonomous transportation systems have the potential to eliminate the “human factor” in accidents by combining the technologies such as IoT, 5G and ML. Computers will not be easily tired, distracted or emotional.

#### 7.7.1.2 Better Tracking Ability

Data gathering is a critical component of responsible infrastructure management. It not only offers complete data for every component of the intelligent transportation system, but it also aids managers in improved tracking maintenance requirements, identifying critical sources of problems and monitoring operations that must be handled.

#### 7.7.1.3 Enhanced Efficiency

Better management translates to more efficient utilization. High quality data is more useful for not only recognizing areas but also for enhancing the efficiency. Perhaps a minor change in rail schedules would result in advanced fill rates, and if the bus stops are distributed, the bus routes will serve the neighborhood in a better way.

#### 7.7.1.4 Cost-Effective

Since an intelligent transportation system is making an efficient use of available resources, results in reduced energy usage and also the costs, preventative maintenance, and minimal resources allocated to vehicular accidents. Riders can save money when public transportation is affordable, and it is efficient to strive with an action of owning the private vehicles.

### 7.7.2 Traffic Management Component

They can receive quick prominence and notifications for accidental-prone and crowdy areas affecting public safety, emergency response systems, traffic on city streets \[[10](#ch7.xhtml#ref7_10)\], and allowing them to interact more effectually with emergency responders and other agencies.

Aside from the previously mentioned benefits of improved administration, safety, and efficiency, there are a number of other advantages \[[8](#ch7.xhtml#ref7_8)\] that the local governments, general public, and around the world which is listed below:

*   Security;
*   Resilience of the supply chain;
*   Environmental points to consider.

#### 7.7.2.1 Security

The susceptibility of smart cities to cyber-attacks is a key concern for skeptics. After all, as the globe becomes more linked, cyber-attacks on key structure such as Internet-connected banking systems and power grids have become practically regular among criminals and even nation states \[[12](#ch7.xhtml#ref7_12)\].

Long before computers, electricity grids, and banks are other essential infrastructure, such as transportation, were susceptible to physical attacks. When network infrastructure and vehicles are integrated, the feature of autonomous is improved which increases the physical risks such as car theft, terrorists utilizing vehicles as weapons, and an intention of holding public transportation as hostages can all be eliminated with the use of cryptography \[[9](#ch7.xhtml#ref7_9)\].

Cyber-attacks, on the other hand, are far easier to fight against the physical threats mentioned above. Cyber-attacks can be mitigated through the techniques presented in [Figure 7.8](#ch7.xhtml#fig7_8).

![Figure 7.8 Techniques involved in the mitigation of cyber-attacks.](images/fig7_8_B.jpg)

[Figure 7.8 Techniques involved in the mitigation of cyber-attacks.](#ch7.xhtml#R_fig7_8)

*   Encrypted communications;
*   Multi-layered security techniques;
*   Virtual private network (VPN) tunnels;
*   Software updates.

This means that smart transportation when incorporated in smart cities can make current public transit safer by minimizing the risk of physical attacks as well as cyber-attacks \[[13](#ch7.xhtml#ref7_13)\].

#### 7.7.2.2 Supply Chain Resiliency

A world-wide catastrophe like the COVID epidemic has demonstrated how sensitive the world’s supply lines once they are interrupted. When the heavy and light vehicle drivers become unwell, the public health is at high risk once they are in transition from one place to another. In these situations, autonomous goods transportation can be used to save the lives of the people.

Projects like Wyoming’s linked car project could pave the way for an autonomous supply chain. It is designed with intelligent city-to-city transportation cum logistics systems to transport crucial products like food and emergency supplies without putting human drivers at risk. Automation, artificial intelligence (AI), and robotics can be used to enhance the human workforce and supply chain with the improvement in safety and security.

#### 7.7.2.3 Environmental Points to Consider

Transportation’s history is inextricably linked to the environment. Transportation has a negative impact on the planet’s resources and atmosphere, from coal consuming steam cars to today’s gas-based combustion engines.

An extent of scientific progress is being made each and every day to identify alternate energy resources needed for power transportation. Smart transportation also helps communities to more responsibly use their existing resources.

Although public or group-based transportation is healthier for the society than using the private vehicles, which will not be extensively used in the United States and in a few other countries due to its inconvenient nature in some areas. Smart transportation solutions, on the other hand, can increase the efficiency of modern public transportation, making it more appealing to a wider range of people. As urban transportation technology advances, successful approaches may be reproduced and expanded to regions that desire to reap the benefits of a smart transportation-based city.

### 7.7.3 Drawbacks of Smart City Transportation Systems

With all of its advantages, some drawbacks may emerge as a result of smart and intelligent based city transportation systems are adopted. These issues primarily revolve around two factors:

*   Power usage; and
*   Data management.

#### 7.7.3.1 Sensors and Power Usage

Smart cities are designed with sensors and all these sensors necessitate power. This will necessitate the use of batteries for sensors linked to moving objects. While the fixed or immovable sensors is potential enough to run on solar power, they will almost always need to be connected to the electrical system of the smart city. It is practically impossible to power up the large number of sensors (Say trillion sensors) needed to convert the whole world into smart cities.

Even with sensors connected to the electrical grid, the quantity of raw material required (e.g.: copper) is far greater than what the global population is used to producing.

#### 7.7.3.2 Data Management

Aside from electricity, there is a lot of discussion about the utilization of internet by a mass population which results in a huge volume of personal data online these days.

Data is acting as the building block or backbone of smart cities. Without it, they would not be able to function. While the majority of the data required is unstructured and unauthenticated in comparison to online data. It will necessitate a behavioral as well as the mental shift among those people. Cars should acquire the positioning data whereas the sensors placed across a city has to passively capture the signals emitted by a smartphone during the day.

If the smart cities integrated with smart transportation needs to prosper in the future, responsible rules and procedures for managing data should be developed.

## 7.8 Working Procedure Of Smart Transportation

### 7.8.1 Vehicle-To-Vehicle Communications In Traffic Control

Public infrastructure and the automotive sector are the two primary categories that smart transportation falls into. When the sensors connected in a network are linked into both the infrastructure and on the vehicles to achieve the goals of remote monitoring and vehicle control and personal safety, these two sectors become “smart.”

Consider a congested city junction. Pedestrians are attempting to cross the street. The traffic flow is controlled by the lamps. Vehicle drivers are frantically attempting to reach their destination. Traditional transportation systems use timers or pressure plates beneath the road or placing the buttons on the curb for the pedestrians to turn on the lighting.

It is the responsibility of both drivers and pedestrians to pay attention to (and obey) traffic lights. However, if any one of the nodes in the network fails, both safety and efficiency suffer. A driver who is inattentive runs a red light. When a pedestrian forgets to press the crossing button, they miss their turn and must wait longer.

All of this, however, changes at a smart intersection. A vehicle can identify pedestrians using a mix of Bluetooth and LiDAR and automatically begin brakes to prevent an accident. Pressure plates and timers cannot pick up individual signals transmitted by automobiles, so streetlights can identify not only the exact number of cars are waiting in a road but also the direction more accurately and effectively. Cars and streetlights can even interact to the point that the car starts moving when the light becomes green, and slows down and stops when the light turns red.

All of this is made possible by the use of technology in transportation, such as the IoT and 5G connectivity speeds enabling real-time activities and remote sensing.

## 7.9 Urban Transportation Technology Examples

### 7.9.1 A Bustling Metropolis

Regardless of the benefits and drawbacks of smart cities, the technique is now available and in use. Smart sensors and adaptive controllers are being used in passenger-information systems \[[11](#ch7.xhtml#ref7_11)\], public transportation dispatch systems and in train networks all around the world and in major parts of the United States. Here are some instances of smart city transportation.

#### 7.9.1.1 Smart Traffic Management in Miami Dade

Southern Pennsylvania Transportation Authority (SEPTA) is a public transportation agency in the state of Pennsylvania (SEPTA) train control with a positive attitude (PTC).

#### 7.9.1.2 Smart Dispatch System (Suburban Mobility Authority For Rapid Transit)

##### 7.9.1.2.1 Advanced Traffic Management System (ATMS) of Miami Dade

With a population of nearly 2.5 million people, Miami Dade County is Florida’s most populous county. The County of Miami Dade is responsible for managing traffic flow throughout the urban region that makes up the city of Miami and its neighboring areas, including the operation of nearly 2,700 signalized intersections. In fact, every year, the number of signalized intersections and mid-block crossings grows by dozens.

The Miami Dade ATMS, which integrates Digi 4G LTE cellular routers as a chunk of the communications structure in county-wide traffic cabinets, is intended to minimize traffic congestion and delays while also increasing mobility.

#### 7.9.1.3 Ptc Septa

SEPTA (Southeastern Pennsylvania Transportation Authority) is in charge of Philadelphia’s light rail, subway, and bus systems. With over 1 million daily riders, these services must be dependable and safe every time a vehicle leaves the station. SEPTA created a positive train control (PTC) system to signal trains, prevent derailments and crashes, and track speed and signal infractions.

With the Digi WR44-RR mobile access router, SEPTA achieve this. This device, when installed aboard a train, enables for remote communications with roadside sensors through a radio link. The device provides signals with train movement data while also receiving information about road closures and other circumstances that can force a plan adjustment. This prevents trains from speeding into a potentially dangerous situation.

#### 7.9.1.4 Smart Dispatch System

It is a computer-assisted dispatching system. In smart cities, public transportation is a crucial area for connected technology innovation. Over 300 buses are managed and dispatched by SMART (Suburban Mobility Authority for Rapid Transit Authority) in Detroit.

Buses are a key component of how people travel around, therefore they must be on time, safe, and free of breakdowns. The city employed three radio towers in an analog radio network, which is dispersed throughout the region to facilitate bus dispatch and location tracking.

They used the Digi WR44 R mobile cellular router when it was time to upgrade. The transition from analog to digital enabled much better tracking and administration. A new innovation allowed SMART to track not just the position of each car, but also its speed and maintenance data.

For society, the smart transportation market holds a lot of promise. People will realize efficiency, cost benefits and safety which comes with contemporary public transit as intelligent transportation systems (ITS) for smart cities become more widely used around the world. It’s fascinating to consider how civilization will engage with their cities in the future, given the new technology that are becoming available.

## 7.10 Transportation Reinforcement Learning (RL)

RL is used to tackle problems requiring consecutive judgments. It works through trial-and-error learning a strategy over time. This structure appears simple, yet it is really sophisticated, and unexpected behavior can develop. In my new book, RL in detail, you may learn more about the specifics of RL.

Transportation, in particular, faces a wide range of these difficulties. Take, for example, driverless vehicles, which is now a separate area. Moving anything or someone from one location to another is essentially a large sequential decision-making problem.

ML, on the other hand, creates models from data. RL is no exception, and it frequently requires additional input because it must explore in order to find optimal solutions, which implies that in all but one situation, behavior is sub-optimal and sometimes catastrophic. There are a variety of technical techniques for promoting safe RL, but most studies start with simulations. Even after proving in the real world, returning to the simulation with new data and learning is beneficial.

But what are the most prevalent transportation issues being addressed?

1.  **Self-Driving Cars:** Autonomous driving presents a number of challenges that are well-suited to RL. Sensing the environment, leveraging information from other vehicles, decision-making, strategic planning, and job execution are all common responsibilities. A lot of work has gone into direct vehicle control, which is commonly used in limited circumstances such as on the highway or for constrained activities like autonomous lane shifting.
    
    Other than direct control, some of the most exciting studies look into other topics. The inverse RL to learn the reward function of an average human driver, rather than attempting to specify a reasonable reward function up front, which is difficult.
    
2.  **Routing and Electric Vehicles:** Electric vehicles (EVs) face extra issues because they require frequent recharging, and the number of recharging sites is restricted. Researchers have explored into using graphs and RL to redistribute EVs for business fleets, such as those used in logistics. Short-term car rental companies have similar challenges.
3.  **Control of Traffic Signals:** All modes of automotive transportation are constrained by signaled intersections. The notion is that the control optimizes vehicle flow, however most existing implementations rely on static logic and extremely simplified traffic distribution assumptions. It’s been proven time and time again that RL outperforms traditional traffic signaling techniques.
    
    RL agent to optimize overall traffic flow and would be far more effective than the current human techniques.
    
    RL presents the option of automating low to no-risk merging of vehicles coming from slip roads in the future, when required speeds can be signaled to individual automobiles or drivers. Even today, where slip roads have some minimal kind of regulation, such as traffic lights, this is conceivable.
    
4.  **Various Modes of Transportation:** RL can be used to improve a variety of modes of transportation, not just those on the road. Train scheduling on both the surface and underground is an evident sequential decision-making task, where rescheduling, re-routing, or canceling trains can help relieve track congestion while minimizing passenger delays. Train shunting and automatic train operations are both scheduling issues, with the extra complexity of determining train speeds and locations. One user who uses RL for shunting activities is the Dutch train company NS.

As a result, it should come as no surprise that another application field is air traffic control. The main reason for this is that manually operating and controlling such a large number of flying objects is getting increasingly challenging. With the growing number of low-altitude drones and vertical takeoff and landing vehicles, a more complex and automated solution, such as RL, is becoming more important.

However, you might be surprised to learn that another prominent RL topic is ship automation. The intersection of two key RL applications, scheduling, and control, is represented by shipping. Because shipping is a commercial activity that exists due to logistical requirements, optimizing the timing and routing of goods to arrive to a destination makes sense. Autonomous ships have lately been studied to increase safety, lower expenses, reduce environmental effect, and combat piracy. Because of their ability to manage complicated and dynamic systems, RL agents may be trusted with the control and navigation of these ships.

## 7.11 Final Thoughts on Transportation Equality

There are lot of issues in directing transportation which are listed as follows:

*   Minimize delay;
*   Maximize flow;
*   Safety issues;
*   Lack of data updation in real-time.

RL paves a way to an entirely different approach to how transportation infrastructure is prioritized:

*   Transportational access to high-quality institutions such as schools, hospitals, libraries, and even markets are linked to educational and health outcomes.
*   Differing racial and socioeconomic groups have different levels of access to metropolitan transportation networks.
*   Governmental agencies can intervene to enhance facilities in underserved areas or to improve transit connections.

It could be as simple as modifying a bus route in terms of implementation with RL to increase social equality by optimizing bus routes. RL has the potential to improve the lives of those who would otherwise lack the ability to shape their own destiny.

RL is a cutting-edge ML technique that optimizes sequential decisionmaking. It has the potential to change the way we travel, move things, and, hopefully, improve the lives of people who are less fortunate. If you’re interested in learning more about RL, I highly recommend my book “Reinforcement Learning: Industrial Applications of Intelligent Agents.” Agents are accomplished with reward and punishment system in RL. The agent is rewarded for good decisions and penalized for bad ones. The agent seeks to minimize the number of incorrect moves while increasing the number of correct ones.

### 7.11.1 Self-Driving Car Applications

Deep RL has been proposed in a number of articles for autonomous driving. There are several factors to consider in self-driving automobiles, including speed limitations in various locations, drivable zones, and avoiding crashes, to name a few.

Motion planning, trajectory optimization, scenario-based learning policies, controller optimization and dynamic pathing for highways are few autonomous driving activities where the technique of RL could be used.

Learning automated parking policies, for example, can help with parking. Q-Learning can be used to change lanes, and overtaking can be done by implementing an overtaking procedure or policy while evading collisions and maintaining a constant speed afterwards.

The AWS DeepRacer is an autonomous racing automobile that was created to put RL to the test on a real-world track. It controls the throttle and direction using a RL model and cameras to visualize the runway.

Wayve.ai has successfully used RL to teach an automobile to drive in just one day. To solve the lane following problem, they developed a deep RL algorithm. They used a deep convolutional network with four convolutional layers and three fully linked layers.

### 7.11.2 Reinforcement Learning (RL) For Industry Automation

Learning-based robots are utilized to execute many jobs in industry reinforcement. Apart from being more efficient than humans, these robots are also capable of performing activities that would be dangerous for humans.

Deepmind’s usage of AI agents to cool Google Data Centers is a wonderful example. This resulted in a 40% reduction in energy consumption. The AI system currently controls the centers completely without the need for human involvement. Data center experts are evidently still in charge of supervision. The following is how the system works:

*   Taking five-minute snapshots of data from data centers and feeding them to deep neural networks;
*   It then forecasts the impact of various combinations on future energy usage;
*   Identifying measures that will result in minimal energy use while adhering to a set of safety standards;
*   Sending these steps to the data center and putting them into action;
*   The local control system verifies the acts.

# Chapter 8 Role of AI and IoT in Intelligent Transportation

PAWAN WHIG^([⁶](#fn6))

## Abstract

The transport industry is being transformed by artificial intelligence (AI). It is now implemented in various transport areas, from helping vehicles, trains, ships, and airplanes to work autonomously, to make traffic flows smoother. In addition to making our lives better, it will help make other forms of travel easier, safer, more effective and smarter. AI-led autonomous transport could for instance help to reduce the human errors that are involved in many traffic accidents. These prospects, though, raise real obstacles, including unforeseen effects and misuse, such as cyberattacks and skewed transport decisions. There are also job consequences and legal concerns related to responsibility for actions made on the spot by AI on humans. Society has accepted the concept of smart transportation and has implemented it broadly. At the same time, smart transportation challenges are emerging, and a new path for its growth is given by the internet of things (IoT). The dream of a smart city administration structure using strong and sophisticated connectivity technology has been realized by IoT-based transport networks. In this chapter, we will concentrate on the smart transportation IoT system and innovations that play a key role in the management system of smart transport. And also, we will look at the role of IoT and AI in the transportation industry and highlight different applications where IoT architecture can be implemented.

## 8.1 Introduction

According to analysts, by 2020, 75% of new cars will have IoT connectivity \[[1](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_1)\]. Although the percentage rise refers to consumer applications, the concept of connected automobiles should pique the interest of other industries like shipping, logistics, and transportation \[[2](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_2)\]. Leaders in these fields would be prudent to prepare for a future in which AI and the internet of things (IoT) alter transportation management \[[3](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_3)\]. The era in which we live, the present, is the most technologically sophisticated in history \[[4](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_4)\]. Advancements in every business area, from customer-facing technology to predictive inventory to transportation management, are coming as a result of these new technologies \[[5](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_5)\].

Smart technology is changing the way businesses operate, and it’s having an impact on your business whether you recognize it or not. Businesses are using AI and IoT at a breakneck pace to streamline processes and optimize data \[[6](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_6)\]. This technology has already made its mark in large cities, and it’s swiftly gaining traction in transportation management.

The era we live in, the present, is the most technologically advanced in history. Advancements in every business area, from customer-facing technology to predictive inventory to transportation management, are coming as a result of these new technologies \[[7](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_7)\].

Smart technology is changing the way businesses operate, and it’s having an impact on your business whether you recognize it or not \[[8](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_8)\]. Businesses are using AI and IoT at a breakneck pace to streamline processes and optimize data. This technology has already made its mark in large cities, and it’s swiftly gaining traction in transportation management \[[9](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_9)\]. Transportation management, among all urban responsibilities, presents an intriguing problem, even for the most advanced AI tools and technologies.

City traffic is a highly dynamic environment in which thousands of participants using various modes of transportation interact in complex ways. Furthermore, decisions must be made in real time to ensure the safety and well-being of all traffic participants. In such an environment, activity planning is a difficult task \[[10](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_10)\]. Fortunately, AI-powered Smart City technologies are already making significant strides in addressing some of the most pressing transportation management challenges \[[11](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_11)\].

## 8.2 Background

All shipments were handled by carriage and horses before Henry Ford developed the first car in 1908 as shown in [Figure 8.1](#ch8.xhtml#fig8_1). Companies had to fight these methods with poor safety and efficiency. When automobiles began to appear on the markets and on roads, people began to use them for the delivery of goods \[[12](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_12)\]. The time spent shipping products and logistics costs have been reduced. Later in the end of the 20^(th) century, a major change in logistics changed the delivery process: software logistics. Since then, technology has taken over multiple tasks, including paperwork and manual workflows, while securing information that is vulnerable \[[13](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_13)\].

![Figure 8.1 First car by Henry Ford in 1908.](images/fig8_1_B.jpg)

[Figure 8.1 First car by Henry Ford in 1908.](#ch8.xhtml#R_fig8_1)

Technology continually disrupts the logistics sector and alters the management of freight, sales, materials, goods, manufacture, and inventory \[[14](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_14)\]. Businesses always look for a solution that will provide information and help to reduce the high cost of logistics operations. As a consequence of the speed, intelligence, and efficacy, new technologies including AI, IoT, and blockchain have been adopted by the logistics sector in order to meet increasing demand and to address complex processes \[[15](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_15)\].

In this chapter, we will discuss the various ways of logistics and transportation development using latest technologies like artificial intelligence (AI) and IoT.

## 8.3 Logistics and Transportation

The field of logistics and transport management requires analysis and accuracy. It monitors goods or materials delivery to customers from suppliers \[[16](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_16)\]. Logistics professionals are responsible for concentrating primarily on the procurement and planning of goods and materials for transport.

The logistics and transport management system mainly handles are follows and the main functions are also represented in [Figure 8.2](#ch8.xhtml#fig8_2):

![Figure 8.2 Main function of transportation management system.](images/fig8_2_B.jpg)

[Figure 8.2 Main function of transportation management system.](#ch8.xhtml#R_fig8_2)

 

*   Request for collection and delivery;
*   Optimization of carrier management pickup;
*   Management of warehouse;
*   Delivery of transit;
*   Reporting and BI.

## 8.4 Request for Collection and Delivery

The client’s role is to provide the carrier with pickup and delivery demands. This is how an interface can be developed for customers to send requests for collection and delivery \[[17](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_17)\].

A new solution to manage goods pickup and supply from scratch cannot be developed. Many logistics software, including NetSuite and McLeod, is available on the market and can be used to manage operations efficiently. The client’s role is to provide the carrier with pickup and delivery demands \[[18](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_18)\].

### 8.4.1 Netsuite

NetSuite is a mainstream coordination stage for the incorporated determining and planning, inventory network and stock, income the executives, client relations and business insight \[[19](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_19)\]. It is a product of Oracle and the UI Interface is look as given in [Figure 8.3](#ch8.xhtml#fig8_3).

![Figure 8.3 UI of NetSuite.](images/fig8_3_B.jpg)

[Figure 8.3 UI of NetSuite.](#ch8.xhtml#R_fig8_3)

 

NetSuite is an online service that provides companies with the ability to manage every important business process in one single system \[[20](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_20)\]. There is no hardware, no high and early license fee, no hardware or software maintenance fees and no complex setups.

Companies use the NetSuite for corporate resource planning (ERPs) and inventory management, financial tracking, e-commerce shopping, and CRMs. This flexible platform is suitable for a variety of business applications \[[21](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_21)\].

In order to reduce their operating costs to increase their competitive advantage, many companies turn to the cloud to deliver their business systems. NetSuite, the Cloud ERP offers a set of cloud-based financial and enterprise resource planning (ERP) and ombudsman ship trading software, which operate in more than 200 countries and territories with over 18,000 customers \[[22](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_22)\].

### 8.4.2 McIeod

McLeod provides lorry software and transport management solutions and manages lorry operations, document imaging, fleet management, automation processes and EDI. To manage pickup and delivery requests, one can use the solutions mentioned above the UI (user interface) of McLeod is shown in [Figure 8.4](#ch8.xhtml#fig8_4). At the point when a client orders pickup or conveyance, the request is shipped off the dispatch, who allots the shipment to different drivers. Transporters should decide if the pickup or conveyance is done on time, just as how often the driver digresses from the booked course. It is feasible to do as such by joining AI and IoT into coordination applications \[[23](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_23)\].

![Figure 8.4 UI of NetSuite.](images/fig8_4_B.jpg)

[Figure 8.4 UI of NetSuite.](#ch8.xhtml#R_fig8_4)

 

McLeod Software is dedicated solely to the transportation industry with an established base of over 1,000 active customers in North America. This means that the needs and the complicated details of carriers, brokers, and freight management companies of all types are fully understood \[[23](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_23)\].

They also run regional offices with training facilities in Salt Lake City, Utah, and Downers Grove, Illinois, and own McLeod Software in Their national Headquarters in Birmingham, Alabama. They employ over 500 professionals, most of whom are developers of software \[[24](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_24)\].

McLeod Software plays a key role in the trucking and brokering industry. They are members and members of over 20 state and regional organizations, in addition to the national organizations ATA, TCA, TIA, AFTC, NTTC, and NPTC \[[25](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_25), [26](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_26) and [27](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_27)\].

In the supply chain operations, Gartner has predicted the use of advanced analysis, IoT, and AI by up to 50% of major global companies \[[28](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_28)\].

### 8.5.1 Predictive Analytics

Effective predictive logistics analytics ensure fleets are received and shifted in due course, to provide customers with shipments as they wish \[[29](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_29)\].

Inserted in trucks, prepares or delivers feed information like motor execution and speed, sensor-enacted resources or IoT gadgets send and model the assessed appearance times and motor flaws to the transporters. For example, telematic information from a vehicle can show its excess speed, position, condition, and time to arrive at the objective \[[30](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_30)\]. The recorded information can be utilized to advise beneficiaries of deferrals alongside load-down, lorries headed towards similar objectives and necessities for item conveyance. This guarantees that postponements and client assumptions are limited \[[31](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_31)\].

Intelligent AI and IoT logistics help ports, shippers, vendors, and agents optimize the use and schedules of resource.

### 8.5.2 Forecasting on the Basis of Inventory

Companies can anticipate demand for shipments and goods throughout the supply chain with logistics demand forecasts. In order to evaluate the demand of capacity based on historical data, including inventory data and order data, logistics companies need to set up a predictor model \[[32](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_32)\].

Since coordination ventures can precisely conjecture the requests for resources and shipments, they can build load limit and give clients popularity merchandise on schedule.

### 8.5.3 Iot And Artificial Intelligence (Ai)-Driven

The application of IoT logistics sensors makes goods tracking more accessible. Data collection and exchange is done using sensors. IoT enables data handling over the network infrastructure remotely \[[34](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_34)\]. The basic structure is given in [Figure 8.7](#ch8.xhtml#fig8_7).

In carriers carrying goods sensors are integrated. Sensor captured data can be converted to use of AI to provide valuable insight.

AI empowered investigation empower shipments to be followed from flight focuses to the objective and send continuous following reports \[[35](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_35)\]. Effective product monitoring provides information, including:

*   Manipulation or robbery while traveling;
*   Time of departure and arrival;
*   Live shipping location;
*   Any differences from the planned route.

Logistics companies are able to improve the collection and delivery of orders with all the information till the delivery.

Future interest that helps item advancement and organic market arranging can be considered typical from the information gathered during the store network. The manufacturing companies can continue optimizing the production of goods when the product is reached at the final destination.

Take the example of foodstuffs processed. The food production status, temperature at which it is stored, and time of delivery can be monitored using different types of sensors. Analysis of hazards and criticisms in order to ensure that food quality isn’t hindered, checklists are used throughout production and delivery procedures \[[36](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_36)\].

In order to enable companies to implement food security solutions, IoT sensors send useful information relating to food in the supply chain. It contributes not only to complying with rules on the safety of food and also to preserving customer faithfulness and confidence by total clearness.

The carrier’s role is also to provide the optimized routes for the organized delivery of the goods and interact with warehouse owners \[[37](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_37)\].

Improvement by shrewd advances, including AI and IOT, can upgrade coordination and decrease the time needed for products assortment and conveyance.

IoT provides information that can be monitored and reported in real time. It is much easier to determine whether delays arise because of bad weather conditions or problems with truck maintenance with remote monitoring capabilities. Thus, the supply chain can effectively monitor the carriers.

#### 8.5.3.1 Role of AI/IOT in Planning

Loads can be allocated to drivers as per their flow area for pickup. Work to pick up goods are assigned to drivers next to the collection site and drivers are informed of the optimized route towards the warehouse \[[38](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_38)\].

For exchange or delivery, trucks and warehouses shall be selected on the basis of, for example, the type of goods to be collected.

IoT sensor-equipped trucks deliver real-time route optimization analysis, reliability, and time reduction. The logistics service providers receive data and alerts captured by the sensors.

IoT sensor information includes:

*   Goods type;
*   The carrier’s real-time location;
*   Abweichungen from the intended route; • Warehouses pick-up/pick-up;
*   Humidity/temperature.

Trucks for pick-up are selected according to the load type to be transported. AI lets carriers accurately take decisions and learn historical patterns. An AI model predicts the correct truck for the collection of goods based on the types of goods to be transferred \[[39](#ch8.xhtml#ref8_39)\].

One can plan pickup of goods into IoT-enabled trucks, for instance, if they have to collect perishable or sensitive goods that require additional care.

### 8.6.1 Preparing Loads

It helps warehouses to efficiently and accurately manage their schedules. Intelligent prediction using AI can be used in time for preparation of load and unload. It helps transporters to decrease the time they need to track down the correct way to enter the stockroom rapidly. AI and IoT therefore also make it easy to plan a free door between logistical companies and warehouses to facilitate better collaboration.

### 8.6.3 Compliance and Security

The unloading/loading of goods from the truck must be avoided. It is essential. To provide security, you can use IoT-enabled locks that allow air brakes to be interlocked with the door of the trailer. Clever locks make it impossible for the truck to leave until unloading is completed. Only when the dock door is closed can trailers leave the warehouse. It can safely maintain your equipment and staff.

The utilization of PC screen is another approach to empower stockroom security. You can introduce IoT cameras in the heap/dump status and burden move stockroom territory. Edge figuring can be applied to shrewd cameras which permit the critical activity dependent on any occasion found during load move tasks.

In the event that any delicate thing separates during dumping, for instance, because of terrible work conduct, stockroom supervisors can be educated about it.

### 8.6.4 Delivery of Transit

It is possible to transform transit operations using AI and IoT-enabled sensors. The GPS sensors installed in trucks are based on the AI model’s real-time assessment of the arrival time.

IoT sensors outfitted with a truck can gather data, including episodes of crash, temperature, and stickiness of products. Logistics companies can track transit operations in real time using real-time data collected through IoT devices. The flowchart of Delivery of transit is shown in [Figure 8.10](#ch8.xhtml#fig8_10).

### 8.6.5 Optimization of Load Exchange

Should the truck be over-burden, or a mishap happen, transporters should stand by quite a while to look for options. Notwithstanding, IoT and AI can assist with decreasing holding up time by catching continuous information and permitting keen activities. AI based models can be executed to advance the heap trade starting with one truck and then onto the next.

For instance, if a lorry meets a mishap, IoT sensors will gather this data and another vehicle is consequently appointed to supplant the heap and send it to the last objective. The truck’s GPS provide details such as latitude and longitude, the location of the truck in real time and the movement of the truck. The carriers can therefore quickly determine a vehicle’s condition during an accident or misapprehension and exchange arrangements can be taken accordingly.

In this manner, new advancements guarantee the smooth travel and supply of products. The subsequent stage is to supply the merchandise to end-clients. The transporter was going to the stockroom to supply merchandise to the last clients. Like the above-mentioned, carriages would be told of the heap arrangement of the empty entryway for pickup of products and stockroom administrators.

Under full security, the load will be transferred to the carrier using IoT-enabled locks and computer vision cameras. The carrier will depart once loading has been completed and the goods delivered to the clients.

## 8.7 Reporting and Bi

Whenever items are gathered and conveyed, organizations would have a complete report, which would incorporate the negative and positive execution patterns in the conveyance of products.

As companies need granular transparency for their transport expenses in order to effectively manage and control them, the demand for business intelligence within logistics and transport is on the rise ([Figure 8.11](#ch8.xhtml#fig8_11)).

![Figure 8.11 Example to show business intelligence and reporting. ¶ Source: Reprinted from https://insightsoftware.com/blog/business-intelligence-vs-reporting-finding-your-bread-and-butter/](images/fig8_11_B.jpg)

[Figure 8.11 Example to show business intelligence and reporting.](#ch8.xhtml#R_fig8_11) ¶ *Source*: Reprinted from [https://insightsoftware.com/blog/business-intelligence-vs-reporting-finding-your-bread-and-butter/](https://insightsoftware.com)

Business intelligence enables data to be converted into valuable data. In the past, the reporting was restricted to data extraction, collection, and transfer from a system in a tablet or database.

But the next level has now been reached by business intelligence where companies are able to produce valuable reports which display all logistics providers data in a scorecard format. The criteria for determining carrier performance are assigned to factors such as pickup and delivery on time, capacity commitments, and driver behavior.

The board likewise can utilize continuous dashboards that give ongoing data and assist clients with taking care of issues when they emerge. They require a day by day and fast outline of what’s going on. Dashboards permit organizations to profit by fast response times since clients don’t need to stand by until somebody makes reports and sends them.

Information that can foresee openings and future dangers on the inventory network/transport network is utilized by organizations as BI and announcing.

Let’s see how suppliers can take an example to improve logistics and supply chain operations through business information.

Let that a shipper has an 89% charge transfer on delivery time, but wishes to attract another 10% of the shipment at the root of the problem. Due to the broken lanes did the delays occur? Does the equipment or carrier have any problems?

It is possible to limit delay factors with business intelligence. Let, a shipper reaches late because of a reason, business intelligence can discover the problem and take action.

BI data facilitates the efficiency of operational decision making for logistics companies and their team. The implementation of BI across shipments can thus lead to end-to-end pick-up and delivery times.

## 8.8 Summarize

Man-made reasoning and the IoT disturb coordination and transport by making coordination tasks step by step more brilliant. Pick-up/delivery delays and lorry capacity problems are expected to become part of the past when AI and IoT are presented.

# Chapter 9 Potential Applications of AI in Transportation

C. N. VANITHA^([⁷](#fn7))

## Abstract

Over the past few hundred years, the transportation sector has witnessed numerous transformations and revolutions, and we are now at a point where significant advancements in the form of artificial intelligence are being made. AI is capturing the attention of transportation executives all around the world, whether it is through self-driving cars for greater dependability, road condition monitoring for increased safety, or traffic flow analysis for greater efficiency. AI is now more available than ever. The whole transportation sector is starting to incorporate AI into its offerings, including automakers, parking garages, cruise lines, and more. In this chapter, we’ll look at a few more instances of artificial intelligence in the field of transportation and how it’s addressing some of the most prevalent and enduring problems in this field.

## 9.1 Introduction

Artificial intelligence (AI) is transforming every aspect of life by allowing robots to learn from their mistakes, adapt, and complete tasks that previously required human cognition. The US government has designated AI as one of its top science and technology priorities. In [Figure 9.1](#ch9.xhtml#fig9_1). The intelligent transportation systems (ITS) Joint Program Office (JPO) designated AI research as a priority area in order to hasten state and municipal agencies implementation of AI to solve transportation issues \[[1](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_1)\]. The objective of this chapter, which is based on a literature analysis, is to identify broad categories of AI-enabled applications that will be used to solve specific transportation challenges and requirements, as well as to summarize existing and potential AI-enabled applications within each category. Time-consuming and repetitive tasks are mechanized by AI-powered systems, which learn over time and eventually conduct key functions and make decisions on their own. Because of this one-of-a-kind potential, transportation firms began investing in AI technology to boost profits and stay ahead of the competition. Although the transportation sector has just lately begun to employ AI in essential tasks, transportation reliability and safety remain a concern. Safety, capacity, pollution, and reliability are all major transportation problems with a lot of room for AI innovation. Despite the challenges, AI is anticipated to reach 3.5 billion dollars in the transportation sector by 2023.

![Figure 9.1 AI in transportation.](images/fig9_1_B.jpg)

[Figure 9.1 AI in transportation.](#ch9.xhtml#R_fig9_1)

 

Transportation AI has progressed and is now being used in a variety of ways. In this study report, there are three major examples:

*   In economic decision, marketing, and governance, AI is being applied. To address the issue of increased demands and high road availability, these are required. To better estimate travel times, traffic patterns, and incidents, more intended to reflect and line detection are used.
*   The use of AI to improve public transportation is also highlighted. Because public transportation is considered a long-term means of mobility.
*   Self-driving cars are now the potential AI use in transportation, with the goal of increasing productive capacity by decreasing the quantity of injury and death.

## 9.2 Transportation Network Structures in AI: Planning, Designing, And Controlling

The network design problem (NDP) involves creating an effective road strategy for transportation planning. If the capacity of present infrastructure changes, it can become a continuous difficulty, a significant problem if extra infrastructure is added, or a mix of continuous and discrete challenges if new infrastructure is introduced. In the 1990s, NNs for transportation planning, design, and simulation had been the subject of research.

A concurrent neural net approach was also used to evaluate the geometric distribution among transport and land planning. Most studies by environment 2019, 11 out of 24 employ Robotic to greater effect among the information as a vast amount of data with powerful algorithms becomes the focus of most studies. This has been providing a projected boost for urban expansion in China based on simulated economic land usage and land cover for many years. It focuses on developing a transportation system management and safety strategy that balances mobility needs with appropriate roadway, railway, and airway distribution \[[2](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_2)\]. They utilized ANN to predict accidents and injuries on a heavily used road between Istanbul and Ankara. In addition, vehicle route planning is necessary to avoid traffic and travel time delays. Many of them came to the conclusion that the ant colony algorithm is one of the most effective ways to solve the vehicle routing problem. While concentrating on applying the BCO algorithm to solve a route and wavelength challenge.

The task comprises picking a path inside an intranet and allocating a wavelength to that path for each linked terminal in order to increase the number of links between the devices in the system. They also focus on modeling and recognizing security infractions utilizing microscopic traffic data, as well as traffic control systems and highway management strategies. They develop and improve the real-time route generating system that relies on traveler choices and determine the optimal routes for public transit customers.

A machine learning (ML) system has also been created to provide signal processing and quick computing analytics capabilities to ITS devices. As IT evolves, data complexity will rise, necessitating the use of ML techniques to discover patterns and characteristics in this data in order to create a more integrated transportation system. At crossroads, for example, a ML model and fuzzy methodologies were utilized to control traffic light systems manually.

The proposed ITS solution, named “Never Stop,” made use of RFID chips and was found to reduce average vehicle wait times. In addition, two NNs systems based on tiny, simulated data were built to better control the road. The first module contains traffic lights, while the second forecasts traffic congestion in the future. It showed an inter NNs system that was evaluated in several interconnection networks to show how Hidden layers may be used to regulate traffic. ANNs can also help with signal traffic control. Based on tiny, simulated data, they built two NNs algorithms to better control the road. AI is an ever-changing study field, with new applications published on a regular basis to take advantage of AI’s skills to improve planning, decision-making, and leadership.

## 9.3 Transportation and Artificial Intelligence (Ai)

Already AI is being utilized in the transportation industry. As AI develops and matures, the variety of jobs in which AI is a significant part unquestionably increase. AI is commonly used in transportation in different ways which are discussed in subsections.

### 9.3.1 Autonomous Vehicles (AVS)

Within the transportation industry, autonomous cars are now a reality. They are the first step toward a replacement mode of autonomous transportation, which previously appeared like a far-fetched concept \[[3](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_3)\]. These self-driving cars are powered by AI’s processing, control, and optimization capabilities. Data transmission and processing in real time are critical in autonomous cars, and any ambiguity in these procedures might result in a catastrophic conclusion. Self-driving taxis have started operating in China, but the operator remains in the vehicle for safety purposes, ready to take operation of the vehicle in case of emergency. In the year 2018, the United States began to employ self-driving trucks. With trucks transporting 65% of goods worldwide. Autonomous transportation is also expected to save about 45% on maintenance and administration expenditures. Waymo has started testing self-driving minivans and trucks on public roads in a limited number of states. These types of initiatives are currently in the early stages of development, with the goal of developing autonomous cars that make passengers safe. Technology advances, driverless vehicles will become more reliable and widespread.

A global study was conducted on one aspect of what makes a cognitive autonomous car desirable to passengers. These were the following aspects:

*   Self-healing;
*   Self-socializing;
*   Self-learning;
*   Self-driving;
*   Self-configuring;
*   Self-Integrating.

### 9.3.2 Public Transportation

The safety of passengers, workers, and drivers has long been a priority in the transportation industry. AI’s application was the autonomous system, which is currently present in nearly every commercial jet and is an essential component of air travel \[[4](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_4)\]. According to the New York Times, just 7 mins of a normal Boeing aircraft are directed by pilots (preflight checks and arrivals), with the remainder handled by an autopilot. Since there are currently autonomous buses that transport people using sensors, cameras, and GPS, AI will have a big influence on public transportation is denoted in [Figure 9.2](#ch9.xhtml#fig9_2).

![Figure 9.2 Autonomous buses.](images/fig9_2_B.jpg)

[Figure 9.2 Autonomous buses.](#ch9.xhtml#R_fig9_2)

AI technology may help us in a variety of ways, including reducing human mistakes in traffic, monitoring compliance with safety rules, reviewing maintenance logs for vehicles and managing transportation in such cases a manner through which lessen the impact of traffic risks in the metropolitan area.

### 9.3.3 Apps for Smartphones

Today, there’s an app for that, and one of them is AI-powered actual traffic data via tools such as ([Figure 9.3](#ch9.xhtml#fig9_3)) google Navigation or Radar \[[5](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_5)\]. These apps make use of smartphone info on location to analyze traffic conditions in your neighborhood and give users with better traffic information. Why would you need an app if the automobile can do everything for you? These applications may soon encounter competition in the field of self-driving cars.

![Figure 9.3 Google navigation.](images/fig9_3_B.jpg)

[Figure 9.3 Google navigation.](#ch9.xhtml#R_fig9_3)

### 9.3.4 Traffic Management

Because of its capacities for processing, control, and optimization, AI May be used to manage transportation systems \[[6](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_6)\]. AI can be used in traffic management to make roads smarter and traffic flow more smoothly. Another significant benefit is the capacity to foresee physical concerns. This might result in excessive traffic or congestion ([Figure 9.4](#ch9.xhtml#fig9_4)).

![Figure 9.4 Traffic pattern.](images/fig9_4_B.jpg)

[Figure 9.4 Traffic pattern.](#ch9.xhtml#R_fig9_4)

Sensors and cameras installed on the roadways capture ([Figure 9.4](#ch9.xhtml#fig9_4)) a huge quantity of data on traffic patterns. Big data and AI technologies analyze this data to uncover traffic trends. The pertinent insights provide commuting with information on forecasts for traffic, accidents, and road closures, as well as recommendations for the quickest routes to their destinations. Siemens Mobility, for example, has developed a prototype AI monitoring system that uses traffic cameras to modify traffic signals depending on actual road jam, reducing traffic issues. Al will be able to decrease duplicate traffic, increase road safety, and shorten wait times in this manner.

### 9.3.5 Detection of an Accident

Several people have attempted to determine the schedule, place, and intensity of an event to aid traffic management in reducing traffic congestion. In these efforts, manual reporting, automated algorithms, and neural networks were all employed. Human-written manual reports might cause a delay in discovering issues and can be costly \[[7](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_7)\]. Algorithms, on the other hand, can use information obtained from sensors along the path to estimate flow parameters before and after an incident. Because it is difficult to utilize an algorithm on major highways due to street road and traffic signals, neural networks techniques were developed. To determine the incidence of an event on a motorway, a neural network method was used to classify it. They discover an excellent software package for real-time detection of all vehicle objects.

Statistical techniques such as the California Algorithm have been used to construct detection algorithms, however utilizing an algorithm on arterial roads is difficult owing to street parking and traffic signals, therefore neural networks approaches have been developed. To determine the occurrence of an event on a motorway, a classification of neural network method was used. They discover a good software program for detecting all vehicle objects in real-time.

Using both virtual and real data, a more specific method called “efficient multiple model particle filter (EMMPF)” was developed to recognize traffic occurrences. Facebook and Twitter can be used to broadcast live events. It means that using Facebook to report traffic accidents on highways and arterial roads is an expense and encourage innovation.

## 9.4 Models of Prediction

With the increasing expansion of ITS, new methods for forecasting traffic data are becoming more important. These technologies are essential for the success of sophisticated passenger data systems, modern road environment, sophisticated transport networks, and heavy truck operations. Historical data obtained from sensors deployed on highways was used to build intelligent prediction systems. Deep learning and Ai technologies use this information to build real-time, short-term, and long-term forecasts. With 90–94% accuracy, the algorithm was able to forecast speed for the next five minutes \[[8](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_8)\]. The inclusion of a contextual level distinguishes it from traditional feed forward networks. This context layer works as a memory box by storing prior data. Ireland’s road traffic control provided the traffic volume data. The future traffic flow’s outcome. This system is designed as a feed-forward deep network utilizing a bottom technique to detect the problem.

When the amount of hidden nodes is double that of input nodes, the network performs better. With a growth rate of 0.5 and a reducing number of iterations, flow accuracy should be between 92% and 98%. The ATOS Scientific Community also created a pattern-based strategy (PBS). Learning algorithm is the first type of pattern recognition, in which branded input is combined with a specific output. Second, unstructured learning, which uses unlabeled data to find similarities and pick the appropriate output; and semi-supervised learning, which employs a small amount of dynamic panel data mixed with a big amount of unlabeled data to do pattern recognition analysis.

The city of Germany submitted data on the rate of flow and velocity and showed that speed forecasting accuracy was 93% and flow rate forecasting accuracy was 86%. As indicated in [Figure 9.1](#ch9.xhtml#fig9_1), there are three phases to building a good prediction model. Six of the 24 convolutional and recurrent neural networks (RNNs) that were tested were helpful in identifying various spatial transport flow characteristics \[[9](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_9)\]. They constructed a basic RNNs for brief predictions using Jordan’s neural network. The context layer distinguishes it from a traditional feed-forward network. This context layer functions as a memory box, holding previously acquired data. The information saved at time (t) is subsequently feed-forward to the hidden nodes, together with the input at period (t) (t–1). As a result, it’s sometimes referred to as “Jordan’s Sequential Network” since it helps the network analyze the subsequence.

The traffic volume data was obtained from Ireland’s road traffic management system. The end outcome is the traffic flow in the future. ATOS Scientific Community also created a PBS. Moderately finding, in which only a small amount of supervised data is paired with a significant amount of unlabeled pattern recognition analysis, and unsupervised learning, in which unlabeled data is used to forecast a structure and select the right output. The city of Berlin submitted data on velocity and rate of flow, and the findings showed that speed forecasting accuracy was 93% and flow rate prediction reliability was 86%.

To create a strong prediction, it is necessary to recognize 3 stages, as shown in [Figure 9.5](#ch9.xhtml#fig9_5).

All data sources must be analyzed and exploited in [Figure 9.5](#ch9.xhtml#fig9_5) in order to integrate the advanced model into the industry.
