Because the model in this phase must be constantly changed and enlarged to deliver the best results, the last phase of [Figure 9.1](#ch9.xhtml#fig9_1) displays real-time asset performance prediction information.

Data was initially grouped based on the daily route choices of the population. To categorize these data, a decision tree is employed, and a support vector machine (SVM) is used to enhance and extract more significant characteristics from the data. They’re both supervised neural networks. To construct a forest, a decision tree employs a huge number of de-correlated decision trees. The greater the number of trees, the higher the accuracy. It operates by categorizing each tree (x) according to its credit value. The forest picks the category with the most votes, and the tree votes for it. SVM, on the other hand, classified the inputs by increasing the distance between them. They obtained data on large-scale ridesharing demand from DiDiChuxing in Guangzhou, China.

This result implies that the suggested model performs better. Using an inter feed-forward neural network, we projected taxi demand in Paris, China. They get their information through the Taxi Probe system, which equips cabs with data-gathering equipment (e.g., Location of the cab). Using 4 hours of historical demand data and 50 layers in the hidden nodes enhanced accuracy rate, according to the findings. Taxi drivers’ performance L1-Norm SVM was used to identify the most essential feature from a cab pattern. Using taxi datasets from New York City, we employed ML algorithms to estimate taxi trip demand. The DNN outperforms other ML algorithms. To get ideal outcomes, the correct architecture must be identified. Finally, AI has the potential to save lives and decrease the effect of urban road accidents. The causes of automobile accidents differ in space and time. This study’s findings demonstrate that this approach is successful and that it may be used to alert individuals about unsafe locations. To determine the risk level of traffic accidents, a Stack DE noise Auto encoder Simulation model was developed.

They discovered that by employing ML techniques, they may identify key hidden designs in previous car accidents. In Arab Emirates sustainable development 2019, 8 out of 24, it was discovered that gender (males have the highest accident rate), age (those between the ages of 18 and 30 are the most likely to be involved in an accident), and collision type and location all were linked to the fatal severity of an accident. Deep learning techniques such as tree structure and MLP were used to identify these factors.

## 9.5 Aviation and Public Transportation AI Applications

Field data collecting has become more dependable and approachable because of advances in computer science. Because public transportation data is voluminous and unstructured, it must be correctly modeled in order to fully exploit it. Aviation AI has been shown to improve the efficiency of aircraft journeys. Technology (ML), software/hardware, and applications may all benefit from AI (Intelligent Maintenance, Flight Route Optimization). When an airplane is landing, the unsupervised ML method is trustworthy for increasing safety \[[10](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_10)\]. In a nutshell, the plane’s safety was analyzed by utilizing the Probabilistic neural network to verify the engine on board (PNN). The network training of the PNN is identical to that of the MLP, with the exception that it uses a back propagation algorithm instead of a linear function, and the RBN’s hidden layer requires more neurons.

To better identify airplane turbulence, they develop an automatic supervised Random Forest approach. This technology can assist the pilot in staying on course, saving money on fuel, and improving air control management. It’s critical to use ANN to improve the gas turbine monitoring system during flights. The sharing economy in mobility has proven desirable in congested urban centers due to its capacity to provide solutions to eliminate single or less occupant personal autos from the road, especially as it contributes to a reduction in inner-city traffic problems.

By adopting new adoptions to lessen traffic congestion and environmental effects, this enables for a win-win approach that gives both quick economic advantages and efficient long-term sustainable solutions. According to the definition of the collaborative consumption, “ICTenabled networks for transfers of products and services drawing on nonmarket logics such as hosting, renting, donating, and exchanging as well as economic logic; renting and sale.” Uber and Airbnb are the market’s pioneers in the sharing economy, which is gaining popularity largely in the transportation and accommodation industries.

It’s becoming more of a shared economy, with businesses attempting to develop business models for new shared mobility services in order to create a better mass transit system and more effectively bridge the demand-supply gap. Majority of customers are particularly interested in on-demand bike, car, and transit sharing. In addition, these shared mobility solutions address a major weakness in “first-mile, last-mile” transit.

### 9.5.1 Urban Mobility With Intelligence

Smarter decision-making based on real-time data and network optimization through effective infrastructure utilization are among the goals of intelligent urban transportation in the future. Importantly, advocating for a better, safer, and healthier transportation system that promotes intelligent connectivity in order to build a long-term, seamless, and environmentally friendly network \[[11](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_11)\]. Autonomous vehicles (AVs), or automobiles that can travel without the help or supervision of a human driver, were on the horizon.

### 9.5.2 Law Enforcement

Sensors and cameras placed along the roads collect a massive amount of data on traffic trends. This data is analyzed using big data and AI technology to discover traffic patterns. Thanks to the beneficial insights, commuters can get information on traffic forecasts, accidents, and road closures, as well as suggestions for the quickest paths to their locations. Siemens Transportation, for example, has developed a concept AI surveillance system based on the traffic sensors that alters traffic lights in real time to alleviate traffic congestion. AI will be able to decrease redundant traffic, increase traffic safety, and shorten wait times in this manner.

### 9.5.3 Predictions for Delay

The delays in transportation, especially air travel, are another challenge. [Figure 9.6](#ch9.xhtml#fig9_6) depicts flight delays, which in the United States might cost up to $39 million \[[12](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_12)\]. According to a research issued by the University of California, Berkeley, the United States is the most prosperous country in the world. AI turns to the air transport for help in order to avoid the costs of flight delays as well as the bad experience of passengers. AI may utilize its abilities to predict anything from indecency to a precise number of technical failures that could cause flight delays, using analysis of data and machine vision to assist and reduce passenger long waits. While AI and ML use and examine real aircraft data, records, and meteorological data, computer vision systems can watch planes on a periodic basis. Computing can expose certain hidden trends, allowing the sector to get important insights on potential delays or cancellations.

### 9.5.4 Taxis By Drone

Drones have already been utilized in delivery methods, but they could soon be included in vehicles as well. Unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) are a unique technology. Strategy for lowering carbon dioxide emissions, road congestion, and infrastructure costs \[[13](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_13)\]. Customers will benefit from drone taxis since they will arrive at their destinations faster and journey time will be reduced to a minimum. When it comes to topics like spatial regeneration and structural infrastructure development in metropolitan areas, drone taxis are frequently a reality. A demonstration of a driverless air vehicle in China, where 17 passengers got to enjoy efficient air mobility for the first time, was one of the most recent examples of drone taxis \[[14](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_14), [15](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_15)\]. Uber made history when it announced a collaboration with NASA to build the technology needed to operate autonomous “flying taxis” ([Figure 9.7](#ch9.xhtml#fig9_7)) a few years ago. Applications based on these use cases are likely to be similar in the future.

## 9.6 The Limitation of Artificial Intelligence (Ai) Techniques

AI approaches have been subjected to a number of critiques. An overview of the transportation sector was presented to them. One of AI’s major flaws is treating ANNs like a “black box.” Without any prior understanding of the system’s fundamental mathematics, the link between input and output is formed \[[16](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_16)\]. The ability of ANNs to generalize when some information is missing from data sets has been called into doubt. They overcame this restriction by using a hybrid method that combines neural networks with other traditional techniques and AI technologies to solve the problem. This need for hybridization to enhance performance, particularly in multiscenarios, is seen as a universal flaw.

The development of AI-based for an effective transportation system is exceedingly tough due to the increase of mechanical intelligence as well as the proper interpretation of human-based information. AI applications in the transportation industry are limited to specific ITS functions like data processing and traffic predictions. It would be more efficient to have AI programs that could manage the entire operation. The accuracy and timeliness of forecasts degrade when AI techniques rely on data obtained using traditional methods such as sensing devices, detectors, and controllers.

The ability to employ AI to discover the optimum solution for Bitmap algorithms (e.g., GA and ACO). Unlike AI raster algorithms, techniques can give a genuine knowledge of the fundamental structure of the issue and the nature of the answer in addition to mathematical computations. In difficult optimization situations where standard mathematical approaches are impractical, the rapid analytical answers provided by these algorithms are preferable to no solutions. Building smart technologies is expensive due to their complexity and ongoing maintenance, and AI-driven systems lack user privacy and transparency, to name a few instances.

We’ll look at a few AI computation complexity constraints in the next section. In a basic algorithmic scenario, the number of data increases at a constant pace as the number of operations increases. In complex algorithm setups, the amount of operations and calculations is expressed in time complexity (X2), logarithmic time (2X), and exponential time (X!). Algorithm complexity is expressed as a P problem or an NP problem. To deal with the complexity of the expanding data density, rapid processing equipment are required. To solve it, a non-deterministic turning machine is employed, and polynomial time methods are used to verify it.

The concept is to use a large number of computers to simultaneously propose all possible solutions to the same problem, with the best solution arriving in polynomial time. There are two types of NP: NP-hard and NP-complete. The NP-hard problem emerges when attempting to create an algorithm that works for all inputs, whereas the NP-complete problem is impossible to fix in constant time and hard to approximate in an optimization problem.

Traffic flow, speed, occupancy, and passenger behavior are examples of these properties. The initial data set is split into various subgroups and spread across numerous parallel processors. The computation will take longer to finish as a result of this. The map-reduce approach can be used on a single computer with multiple cores or CPUs. Each CPU or core will handle a portion of the massive datasets in order to generate the best reaction and speed up AI learning algorithms.

They demonstrated that it is a successful approach for adapting when identifying abnormalities in traffic occurrences for a large data set. This works by splitting the data into subgroups and analyzing each set separately for parallel processing. To find the bus arrival time, use map-reduce. To split the running duration of a bus, they utilize the Clustering K-Mean method.

The data may be clustered into k groups using the K-Mean method. It runs several times in order to identify multiple suitable configurations. Deep learning was used in picture identification jobs for the healthcare industry, as well as Facebook face recognition features, to arrive at this figure. ML \[[17](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_17)\] AI technology is helping the automotive, banking, and data mining industries better their operations. In addition to detecting patterns among data for future study, deep learning will see a significant increase in value, reaching $10.2 billion by 2025.

They talked on how AI’s value in the travel and transportation industry may be expanded from a corporate, economic, and societal standpoint. AI’s current deep learning neural networks, as demonstrated in [Figure 9.8](#ch9.xhtml#fig9_8), can provide better value than previous techniques. Significant savings can be made in two important areas: travel and transportation-related services. AI techniques could be used to find the best and fastest route for the convenience of modes of transport and delivery businesses.

![Figure 9.8 Benefits of AI-assisted performance.](images/fig9_8_C.jpg)

One European company was able to identify real-time truck performance and driver behavior by analyzing data from road sensors \[[20](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_20)\]. This resulted in a 15% reduction in gasoline costs as well as a 15-minute reduction in delivery time. By analyzing the weather and traffic congestion with AI algorithms, airlines can reduce the costs of cancellation.

Reduced traffic congestion, optimum public transit scheduling for consumers, self-driving car user safety, and enhanced air quality are among the additional advantages \[[18](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_18)\]. The value of the travel industry grows from 62% to 128% on average, while transport and logistics increases by 89%. Deep learning values will rise as AVs become more common. According to estimates, if 30% of vehicles in Australian cities are self-driving by 2030, the cost of congestion will drop from $38 billion to $26 billion. ## 9.7 Advantages of Using AI in Transportation

Despite the fact that the usage of AI varies, between locations, the best fit between AI and transportation comes naturally. Adoption of these technologies may have a huge influence on the whole sector \[[17](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_17)\]. Increased usage of AI will result in lower labor costs and better profitability, with fully automated fleets addressing the issue of long driving hours and rest stops. AI may additionally have a significant influence on road Accidents and safety. Driving in the dark might be a dangerous problem, but clever unmanned vehicles can help greatly. Autonomous cars that can run without human intervention can let drivers sleep while avoiding accidents. AI techniques allow us to estimate transportation utilizing travel information and data about current city activity, as well as automate the suggestion of other routes, making transport management more efficient. AI might benefit complex infrastructures and many elements of collaboration chains, such as an optimum travel schedule, reduced waiting time, real-time traffic detection for route changes, and so on. Data analytics could also aid in improving logistics transport systems and improved security. There are many more advantages to mention, but because AI is still developing, the advantages will continue to evolve ([Figure 9.10](#ch9.xhtml#fig9_10)).

![Figure 9.10 Advantages of using AI.](images/fig9_10_B.jpg)

[Figure 9.10 Advantages of using AI.](#ch9.xhtml#R_fig9_10)

## 9.8 Drawbacks of AI in Transport

Although AI has several advantages for every sector, there are also disadvantages to consider, such as increasing transparency in AI decisionmaking or safety concerns with autonomous cars. Job flow is one of the disadvantages, since it has become a big concern for truck or taxi drivers, as well as other industry participants \[[19](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_19)\]. Despite the efforts of social specialists to explain that employment abilities may be transferred to other industries, conflicts persist. All implementations might have downsides, as developing nations would have a difficult time implementing such solutions since their infrastructure will not be able to provide upkeep. Transportation firms also suffer potential AI disadvantages is shown in [Figure 9.11](#ch9.xhtml#fig9_11), since transportation expenses account for up to 10% of a company’s income. To remain competitive in their industry, all existing businesses will need to develop and implement AI technologies, and the enormous costs of manufacturing, fixing, and sustaining such sophisticated machinery can be too expensive. Companies introducing If AI to transportation \[[21](#ch9.xhtml#ref9_21)\] wants to win consumer trust, they must take AI’s ethics seriously. AI technology has a long way to go before it approaches human intellect.

![Figure 9.11 Disadvantage of ai in transport.](images/fig9_11_B.jpg)

[Figure 9.11 Disadvantage of ai in transport.](#ch9.xhtml#R_fig9_11)

## 9.9 Conclusion

AI has progressed from a futuristic concept to a part of our everyday life that we utilize without even realizing it. Our mobile applications, social networking feeds, and the way Grammarly evaluates our grammatical errors all contain AI. For a while, the transportation sector has employed certain AI solutions, but it won’t be long until AI is used more extensively in transportation and logistics. It’ll only be a matter of time until we witness the bright future made possible by AI it becomes more subtle with time.

# Chapter 10 AI Techniques for Future Smart Transportation

S. ANBUKKARASI and C. R. DHIVYAA

Assistant Professor, Department of Computer Science and Engineering, Kongu Engineering College, Tamil Nadu, India

## ABSTRACT

Smart transportation system (STS) is an emerging area of research, and it draws the attention of academicians and Industrialists. It contributes various services to end users, including road safety and accident prediction, traffic prediction and control, and vehicle control and monitoring. The environmental pollution, heavy traffic, unpredictable congestion increases the complexity of the STS. Henceforth, the future STS is in the position to provide solutions for all these problems. It paves the way to enhance congestion control, CO[2] emission control, user security, etc., as part of the transportation services. The increase in population and the transportation needs of the individual makes the Intelligent Transport System embrace various techniques of Artificial Intelligence (AI). In this chapter, we mainly focus on the various artificial techniques for future smart transport management systems. In the first section, the basic existing transportation system and their problems are discussed. The second section discusses the different kinds of AI technologies which deal with smart transportation. The third section discussed the emerging AI technologies for the future smart transportation services. The last section discusses some of the new AI technologies which handle the future smart transportation services.

## 10.1 Existing Transportation System

The process of transporting goods, people or something else from one place to another place is known as transportation. The increase in population makes the transportation problem a tedious one. All over the world, People use motor vehicles in billions for transportation purpose in day-to-day use. The vast usage of these vehicles creates so many problems in managing transportation efficiently. In this section, we discuss the existing transportation system and the problems we face due to this system. The transportation system is considered to be smart when it is supposed to do all or any one of the following tasks:

*   Pollution control;
*   Traffic management system;
*   Driver assistance system;
*   Navigation and vehicle information system;
*   Accident control.

For addressing these issues, smart transportation system (STS) should involve many techniques such as real time data creation, communication, data mining and AI, ML and database management. By using all these technologies, STS provides efficient solutions for the problems discussed above.

The application of STS can be given in broad category as given below:

*   Accident prediction and road safety;
*   Vehicle control;

The function of STS is divided into sub tasks as given in [Figure 10.1](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_1). STS deals with real time data which may be collected from various sensors.

![Figure 10.1 Tasks of STS.](images/fig10_1_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.1 Tasks of STS.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_1)

### 10.1.1 Traffic Prediction and Control

Many works have been carried out so far in smart transportation which deals with one or more problems of transportation. Genetic algorithm (GA) is used for controlling the movement of traffic by controlling the flow route. This algorithm helps to adjust the timings of the signal lights. With the simulation result, they showed that air pollution assessment can be automated \[[20](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_20)\]. Traffic control is achieved by GA by predicting the time at which vehicles are usually stopped at intersections \[[8](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_8)\]. Vehicleto-infrastructure (V2I) communication is helpful in identifying the time for vehicles to arrive at the intersections. The data is collected from the vehicles themselves and GA helps to determine the time to be delayed for arriving at the intersection. By this means, the system updates each vehicle, and the vehicle can make the plan for the speed profile.

An intelligent traffic control system is proposed in Ref. \[[11](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_11)\] with radio frequency identification (RFID). RFID tag is placed in the vehicles which cannot be easily removable, and it can be taken by RFID readers. Number of vehicles at particular time in the traffic can be counted by RFID reader which gives the network congestion in a given time. Based on this information, the duration of green light can be decided. The authors claimed that usage of RFID tag gives the best results in traffic control management. The basic architecture of smart traffic monitoring methodology is depicted in [Figure 10.2](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_2) as discussed in Ref. \[[4](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_4)\].

![Figure 10.2 Basic design model of smart traffic prediction system.](images/fig10_2_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.2 Basic design model of smart traffic prediction system.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_2)

The other technique known as expert system (ES) is used to control the traffic \[[12](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_12)\]. The modified algorithm known as belief rule based expert system (B-RBES) is used to handle the traffic signal based on the uncertainties. The hand-crafted defined rules are used to make the decision in controlling the traffic signal. The major drawbacks of these kinds of rule-based system are the extensive list of rules to be accompanied by the system for the predictions.

### 10.1.2 Vehicle Control

Vehicle control system (VCS) obtains the input parameters from the outside environmental variables, vehicle positioning and sensor information. It processes all these input variables and control brake, steering, and acceleration of the vehicle. VCS mainly focuses on the comfort level of Passenger and driver, vehicle safety, efficient usage of fuel, etc. The general structure of VCS is given in [Figure 10.3](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_3). GA is proposed for charging the electrical vehicles \[[13](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_13)\]. This work mainly focuses on the efficiency of the charging control system. The system is designed by the considering the power constraints. For vehicle monitoring system, a system is prosed to provide exact GPS information \[[18](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_18)\]. The quality of GPS positioning is monitored by integrity check at each level. Map matching process is improved by checking the integrity of velocity using Doppler information of GPS. Fuzzy logic is used for controlling the vehicle along with the human Intelligence for controlling the vehicle’s direction and speed \[[22](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_22)\] In their proposed work, they used global positioning system (GPS) and artificial vision with wireless local area network (WLAN).

![Figure 10.3 Vehicle control system.](images/fig10_3_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.3 Vehicle control system.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_3)

ES is applied in vehicle driving simulator (VDS) in which users can be able to feel the driving by upgrading the database known as knowledge base (KB).

### 10.1.3 Accident Prediction and Road Safety

Accident prediction and road safety system of STS covers the following criteria:

*   Prevention of road accidents;
*   Analyzing the causes for road accidents;
*   Alleviate the reason for accidents so that it improves the survival of the drivers and passengers in the vehicle.

Accidents are caused by various reasons which involves careless of drivers, road condition, negligent driving of other drivers, etc. To avoid such crucial accidents, it is necessary to gather the input values from the in-vehicle sensors and process those values to alleviate an accident, predict the accident, etc.

Using the external factors of the accident, the severity of the injury for the driver and passengers are predicted in Ref. \[[24](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_24)\]. One of the ML techniques known as Artificial Neural Network is used for predicting the severity. Disaster management is improved by incorporating intelligent decision support system in Ref. \[[14](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_14)\]. The system gathers the live data from various sensor networks. It integrates various data such as traffic, road network, and meteorological data for giving intelligent transport platform by implementing intelligent models and algorithms. When the accident is occurred, existing resources are allocated in optimal way using multiobjective GAs in Ref. \[[17](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_17)\]. The major aim of the work is to reduce the severity of the injuries.

The general workflow of STS for accident prediction and road safety is given in [Figure 10.4](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_4).

![Figure 10.4 Transportation system for accident prediction and road safety.](images/fig10_4_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.4 Transportation system for accident prediction and road safety.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_4)

An ES based on web is developed to reduce the collision between the pedestrian and vehicles. Fuzzy logic system in terms of toolbox is embedded in MATLAB by investing many parameters including residential volume, traffic density, Speed of the vehicle. Web based graphical user interface (GUI) is developed to evaluate the system. Many algorithms such as ES, fuzzy system are used to identify the fatigue level of the driver to predict the accident. Thus the system alerts the driver and avoids the accident.

## 10.2 Various Ai Technologies for Smart Transportation System (STS)

One of the major reasons of immediate death around the earth is road accidents. Traffic accidents are a major threat to public health issues and also it causes economic damage to our nation. The reasons for road accidents are raising the population, growing number of vehicles all over the region and it increases the number of wounds in traffic accidents. Hence, the advancement of technology is crucial for improving the road safety by avoiding the possible road accidents. One of the emerging technologies is AI which has been applied for improving the performance of various applications of industries and business in real time. In recent days, AI techniques are needed to solve the transportation problem such as improving travel demand, environmental degradation, CO[2] emissions, safety measurements and the AI techniques are implemented to enhance the intelligent transportation systems (ITS). There are various Artificial Techniques available to address the issues and problems of transportation like safety management, traffic management, mobility management, accident prediction and public transportation management. The several AI techniques are applied in intelligent transport systems, and it is shown in [Figure 10.5](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_5).

![Figure 10.5 AI-based techniques used in intelligent transport systems.](images/fig10_5_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.5 AI-based techniques used in intelligent transport systems.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_5)

### 10.2.1 Artificial Neural Network

The network contains neuron layers which are classified into three layers namely input, output, and hidden layers. Input layer gets input data from the user, the hidden layer acts as a middle layer between input and output layers and this layer performs all mathematical computations, at last output layer provides appropriate outcome for the given user’s input data. In an intelligent transport system, the ANN model is applied for classifying the congestion level, predicting the traffic flow in urban areas, detecting accidents, detecting the location of vehicles, estimating the speed of vehicles on the road and so on. It provides a better solution for planning, scheduling, and developing the traffic signals for public ITS by analyzing the congestion level of the traffic on the road.

The ANN model consists of data collection, transformation, and information processing units. In the data collection unit, all the traffic data are collected using sensors by capturing the number of vehicles and speed of vehicles on the road. The collected data are aggregated and sent to the remote data analysis. The average speed of the vehicles and traffic density of the road are calculated using the traffic raw data which are collected from the different fields. Finally, the information processing unit sends the processed traffic data into ANN model for predicting the congestion level of the traffic on the road. [Figure 10.6](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_6) depicts the basic artificial neural network model for intelligent transportation system.

![Figure 10.6 ANN model for intelligent transportation system.](images/fig10_6_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.6 ANN model for intelligent transportation system.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_6)

### 10.2.2 Fuzzy Logic

Fuzzy logic is one of the AI methods where it takes decision as like human’s decision making and it provides intermediate possible values between yes and no which is based on “If-Then” rules. In an intelligent transportation system, the fuzzy logic \[[19](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_19)\] is suited to address the problems of transportation like traffic prediction, traffic control, finding alternate routes and capturing the driver behavior. The basic model of fuzzy logic is shown in [Figure 10.7](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_7). The fuzzy model consists of fuzzifier, intelligence (decision-making unit), rules and defuzzifier. The fuzzier performs the fuzzification process that transforms the crisp inputs of the system into fuzzy sets. The intelligence unit mimics the process of human reasoning based on the If-Then rules defined by experts. The fuzzy set attained by inference engine is transformed by defuzzifier into a crisp value. Some of the traditional existing controllers are not suitable for addressing the dynamic issues such as weather condition, road condition, and traffic jams. The fuzzy logic has the ability to enhance the existing traffic controllers to overcome the problem of dynamic conditions effectively. For instance, if speed is low and distance is low then break rate is low. The fuzzy logic takes the decision based on fuzzy If-Then rule as like human observation.

![Figure 10.7 Fuzzy logic basic model.](images/fig10_7_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.7 Fuzzy logic basic model.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_7)

### 10.2.3 Genetic Algorithm (Ga)

The popular artificial technique is GA \[[20](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_20), [21](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_21)\] which will be helpful for STS. The GA is efficient for guiding the driver to the target location by providing the information about the traffic on the road.

[Figure 10.8](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_8) depicts the sequential approaches of the GA, and it is described as below:

![Figure 10.8 Sequential approach of genetic algorithm.](images/fig10_8_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.8 Sequential approach of genetic algorithm.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_8)

*   Preparing the initial value of the population. Each chromosome contains number of genes;
*   Evaluating fitness function for each chromosome in the population;
*   Cross over operation is responsible for interchange the information between genes of the chromosome and it produces the new offspring;
*   Mutation is applied to maintain the genetic divergence from one generation to another generation and it is also responsible for the new information;
*   Calculate the fitness function for the new generation by computing the crossover and mutation process;
*   Select the best chromosome among the other chromosomes and transfer to the next generation;
*   Repeat the process until the condition is met.

The GA can be able to produce an approximate solution to address the problem of intelligent transport systems effectively.

### 10.2.4 Expert Systems (Ess)

In AI, the ES is a program which imitates the decision making capability of human experts based on predefined knowledge. It uses a knowledge procedure to solve the complex problems by utilizing human knowledge for analysis. It is a rule-based approach. The ES \[[6](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_6)\] is a crucial technique for vehicle maintenance, external vehicle’s control in transit, vehicle scheduling and traffic signal control. The basic architecture of the ES is depicted in [Figure 10.9](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_9).

![Figure 10.9 Basic model of expert system.](images/fig10_9_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.9 Basic model of expert system.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_9)

The ES consists of several components listed below:

1.  **Inference Engine:** It performs actual reasoning process of knowledge, and it solves the problem by combining the information obtained by the KB and by the user. It is the heart of the ES.
2.  **Knowledge Base (KB):** It is the repository of domain specific knowledge collected from the experts through acquisition module. It comprised of rules, facts, and other knowledge for providing the problem-solving solution by utilizing the AI concepts.
3.  **Knowledge Base Acquisition Module:** It provides the knowledge to the knowledge database. The experts can enter and edit the set of rules (knowledge) in an ES. It gathers the intelligence information from the human experts and stores it into the KB.
4.  **Explanation Module:** It explains the reasoning process, provides solutions and other information to the client. It also helps the experts to find the ability of the system’s reasoning in order to reach a goal.
5.  **Client Interface:** The user can access the system through the client interface. It acts as a mediator tool that provides a connection between the user and the system.

### 10.2.5 Swarm Intelligence System

It is an AI system which consists of agents interacting locally with each other in their environment. Ant colony optimization (ACO) \[[10](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_10)\] and bee colony optimization \[[9](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_9)\] (BCO) are examples of swarm intelligent systems. ACO is an AI algorithm that contains a set of artificial ants to identify the optimal solution for a given problem based on the ant’s foraging behavior. BCO is also an AI technique to detect good solutions for the problem based on the behavior of honeybees. ACO and BCO is a suitable model for traffic control systems to minimize the time delay, average waiting time and congestion level of traffic.

## 10.3 Emerging Ai Technologies for the Future Smart Transportation Services

### 10.3.1 Deep Learning Techniques

Deep learning is a sub class of ML which deeply analyzes the huge amount of data generated by devices and provides a better solution for the complex problem. Hence, deep learning techniques are suitable for addressing the problems in ITS and it also helps for predicting performance of traffic, detecting lane, identifying the best routes for transportation of tangible goods, preventing accidents, finding the objects near vehicles to improve the safety of transportation system, solving all the related issues of transportation system in an efficient manner.

The different deep learning techniques are listed below:

*   Convolutional neural network;
*   Recurrent neural network;
*   Long short-term memory;
*   Bi-directional long short-term memory; • Gated recurrent unit (GRU);
*   Attention based GRU.

### 10.3.2 Convolutional Neural Network (Cnn)

The network \[[2](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_2), [3](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_3)\] contains 5 layers namely input, convolution, pooling, fully connected and output layers. The input layers are used to accept the inputs from the user, the convolution and pooling layers are used to detect the features from the given input, and finally, the fully connected and output layers are acted as a classifier to predict the class depends on the features. In intelligent transport systems, the convolutional neural network (CNN) is suitable for traffic sign identification, classification of vehicle, detecting congestion level of traffic and detecting pedestrian on the road. [Figure 10.14](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_14) depicts the basic architecture of CNN.

![Figure 10.14 Architecture of CNN.](images/fig10_14_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.14 Architecture of CNN.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_14)

In CNN, the activation function is one of the important factors where it decides which neuron will be activated and transferred to the upcoming layers. The various activation functions are shown in [Figure 10.15](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_15).

![Figure 10.15 Activation functions.](images/fig10_15_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.15 Activation functions.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_15)

There are several CNN models available to upgrade the system’s performance. The best model will be selected based on the performance to predict the output class effectively. The different architectures of CNN are listed in [Figure 10.16](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_16).

![Figure 10.16 Different models of convolutional neural network.](images/fig10_16_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.16 Different models of convolutional neural network.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_16)

### 10.3.3 Recurrent Neural Network (Rnn)

Recurrent neural network (RNN) is a kind of supervised deep learning technique which is used for capturing the sequences of data dynamically. In RNN \[[1](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_1)\], the output of the present input depends on the computation of the previous step; it copies the output and sends back to the RNN.

The recurrent network has an internal memory to remember the past computations and it uses two inputs such as the current input and the recent previous. RNN contains a sequence of networks where it trains each neuron one by one with back propagation. In intelligent transport systems, RNN is crucial to process the sequential traffic data for traffic prediction, route prediction and monitoring the vehicle’s speed on the road. The basic structure of RNN is shown in [Figure 10.17](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_17).

![Figure 10.17 Structure of RNN.](images/fig10_17_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.17 Structure of RNN.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_17)

### 10.3.4 Long Short-Term Memory

LSTM \[[2](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_2), [3](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_3)\] is an extension of RNNs where it expands its internal memory for the storage of past computations, and it remembers the input data over a long period. It can perform all the operations as read the information, write the information and delete the information from its internal memory. The RNN suffers from the vanishing gradient point which degrades the performance of the network. Hence, the LSTM is introduced to give a suitable solution to the vanishing gradient problem.

In each cell of hidden layers, there are 3 gates presented in the LSTM network. These gates are used to control the information flow that is needed to find the output in the neural network. The gates are listed below:

*   Input gate which determines the new input;
*   Forget gate deletes the details if it is not needed;
*   Output gate shows the output at the present time stamp.

In ITS, the LSTM is significant for predicting traffic flow on the road, providing traffic information service and congestion prediction.

### 10.3.5 Bi-Lstm (Bi-Directional Long Short-Term Memory)

RNN remembers the past information by incorporating hidden layers in traditional neural networks. For time series problems Bi-LSTM (bi-directional long short-term memory) is very useful. It predicts the next output based on the past inputs. It achieves it by involving gates in the network system. In Bi-LSTM, two independent RNNs are connecting together to form a network. In this way, it remembers the forward as well as backward information about the given input at each time step ([Figure 10.18](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_18)).

![Figure 10.18 Bi-LSTM network.](images/fig10_18_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.18 Bi-LSTM network.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_18)

The foundation of Bi-LSTM is long short-term memory (LSTM) which consists of 3 different gates known as input gate, output gate and forget gate. It uses not only the candidate values but activation values for processing the input values. It produces two output values such as activation value and candidate value.

A candidate value is calculated by:

  
*C*^(*N*) = tanh (*W*[*c*]\[*a*^( < *t* − 1>),*x*^( < *t*\>)\]+*b*[*c*])  

where; *W* is the weight values for the input; *a* is the activation; *x* is the input; and *b* bias.

The output of the Bi-LSTM network *ŷ* at the given time t is obtained from both activations know as forward and backward as:

  
$${\\hat{y}}^{< t >} = g\\left( W\_{y}\\left\\lbrack {\\overset{\\rightarrow}{a}}^{< t >},{\\overset{\\leftarrow}{a}}^{< t >} \\right\\rbrack + b\_{y} \\right)$$  

In this equation:

  
$$\\begin{array}{l} \\left. {\\overset{\\rightarrow}{a}}^{< t >}\\rightarrow Forward\\, Activation \\right. \\\\ \\left. {\\overset{\\leftarrow}{a}}^{< t >}\\rightarrow Backward\\, Activation \\right. \\\\ \\end{array}$$  

As Bi-LSTM has two recurrent units it learns the past information as well as future information to predict the output. Bi-LSTM is one of the emerging techniques for many ML problems. This technique to be applied for various applications of STS.

### 10.3.6 Gru (Gated Recurrent Unit)

Gated recurrent unit (GRU) is similar to LSTM as both uses gates for processing the data by controlling them. The only thing missing in GRU is cell state. It uses only hidden state. This is the reason why it is easy so easy to train GRU than LSTM. The basic model of GRU is given in [Figure 10.19](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_19).

![Figure 10.19 GRU model. ¶ Note: Xt is the given input at timestamp t; Ht–1 is the hidden state of timestamp t–1; and Ht is the hidden state at time stamp t. The current state Ht, will be passed as input in the next timestamp.](images/fig10_19_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.19 GRU model.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_19) ¶ *Note:* X[t] is the given input at timestamp t; H[t–1] is the hidden state of timestamp t–1; and H[t] is the hidden state at time stamp t. The current state H[t], will be passed as input in the next timestamp.

GRU consists of two gates, unlike three gates in LSTM. It consists of:

1.  **Reset Gate (Short Term Memory):** Reset gate acts as short-term memory for the hidden states. The equation for reset gate is given below:
    
      
    *r*[*t*] = *σ*(*x*[*t*]\**U*[*r*]+*H*[*t* − 1]\**W*[*r*])  
    
    Since the sigmoid function is used for reset gate, the value of r[t] will be between 0 to 1. *U[r] and W[r]* are known as weight parameters.
    
2.  **Update Gate (Long Term Memory):** Update gate is used for long term memory access in GRU. The equation is given below:
    
      
    *u*[*t*] = *σ*(*x*[*t*]\**U*[*u*]+*H*[*t* − 1]\**W*[*u*])  
    
    *U[t] and W[u]* are weight parameters for update gate. GRU works in two steps. The first thing is it has to find the candidate state; the second step is to find the hidden state.
    

The candidate state is identified by the given equation:

  
*Ĥ*[*t*] = tanh (*x*[*t*]\**U*[*g*]+(*r*[*t*]\**H*[*t* − 1])^∘*W*[*g*])  

The reset gate output is multiplied by the input and hidden state of the previous time stamp and finally tanh function is applied on it. The output is hidden state of the candidate value.

#### 10.3.6.1 Hidden State

After the candidate state the current hidden state H[t] is generated. The purpose of update gate is to create a hidden state. The new information from the candidate state and historical information from the previous state are controlled by the single gate whereas in LSTM, it is done by implementing two gates. This feature makes GRU simple than any other sequence network model. The equation for calculating hidden state is given below:

  
*H*[*t*] = *u*[*t*] \* *H*[*t* − 1] + (1−*u*[*t*]*Ĥ*[*t*])  

From the equation, it is known that hidden state purely depends on the past information as well as the present information. The first term in the equation points to the information from the previous state and the second term in the equation specifies the information from the current state. So, the value of u[t] is near to 0, then the hidden state will purely depend on the current state and not on the historical information. In this case, the second part will be 1, hence the hidden state relies on the information from the candidate state only. It is clear that the value of u[t] is so crucial in calculating the hidden state \[[24](#ch10.xhtml#ref10_24)\].

### 10.3.7 Attention-Based Gru

Initially attention mechanism in neural networks is introduced for encodedecoder based architecture for handling natural language processing (NLP) tasks.

Later it has been extended to other ML tasks to increase the learning capability of the system. Distance between the input sequences will not affect the dependencies while using attention mechanism. In [Figure 10.20](#ch10.xhtml#fig10_20), Bi-LSTM model is used with attention mechanism. This technique can be incorporated with any other neural networks.

![Figure 10.20 Attention mechanism.](images/fig10_20_B.jpg)

[Figure 10.20 Attention mechanism.](#ch10.xhtml#R_fig10_20)

*Note:* S[t–1] is the previous input sequence; S[t] is the current input sequence; h is the hidden state; and X is the current input.

The hidden state vectors represent the input and output sequences.

  
$$h\_{j} = \\left\\lceil {{\\overset{\\rightarrow}{h}}\_{j}^{T};{\\overset{\\leftarrow}{h}}\_{j}^{T}} \\right\\rceil^{T}$$  

The vectors h[1], h[2], …, h[T] represents the number of words in the input sequence. Here the weights are calculated by feed forward networks. The context vector C[i] is generated by the weights obtained from the sum of annotations.

  
$$C\_{i} = \\sum\\limits\_{j = 1}^{T\_{x}}\\alpha\_{ij}h\_{j}$$  

SoftMax function is used to create weights *α[ij]*. Softmax function represents a function which converts the vector of x-values into a vector of x-values whose values will be summed upto 1. The input values can be positive number, negative number or even more than the value of 1. But after the SoftMax function is applied to the values, it would be changed to the value between 0 and 1. Hence it will be in terms of probabilities.

  
$$\\alpha\_{ij} = \\frac{\\exp\\left( e\_{ij} \\right)}{\\sum\_{k = 1}^{T\_{x}}\\exp\\left( e\_{ik} \\right)}$$  

The above equation is used to calculate the weights of the network.

  
*e*[*i**j*] = a(*S*[*i* − 1],*h*[*j*])  

*e[ij]* represents the output score of feed forward network. Thus, attention mechanism boosts up the learning capability of the neural network.

These are the some of the emerging technologies which are applied for STS to handle all the transportation issues. The system reads the data from the various sensors using IoT technologies. After collecting the data from the sensors, various ML methods which are discussed above are applied, and the decision is made. These technologies bring the intelligent transportation system.

## 10.4 Summary

In this chapter, we discussed various artificial techniques for giving smart solutions for the transport system. Initially we discussed about the existing transportation system and their problems. Then in the second section, we discussed the different kinds of AI technologies which deal with smart transportation. The last section discusses some of the new AI technologies which handle the future smart transportation services. Now a days, deep learning techniques gives solutions for all sort of classification problems. Hence various deep learning techniques such as RNN, BiLSTM, GRU can be used to provide solution for STS. To increase the accuracy of the system, the model’s parameters can be tuned manually or automatically.
