# Chapter 11 AI-Based Green Transportation: A Sustainable Approach

KABEER TIJJANI SALEH^([⁸](#fn8))

## Abstract

Current urban societies are characterized by having been polluted on a daily basis due to increase in urbanization activities, industrialization, and transportation. In an attempt of having clean, healthy, sustainable, and pollution free urban environment, the concept of green buildings, green transportation or rather sustainable transportation is discussed in this chapter. Green transportation unveils the ability of the available transportation modes in our environment to produce a renewable source of energy that is clean, defendant, and has no harmful effect to the inhabitants. This green transportation aimed in achieving sustainability in urban transportation, urban climate, social lifestyle and environmental management in general through artificial intelligence (AI) sustenance. AI is one of the components of green transportation as it refers to the recreation and application of human intelligence in machines that will be programmed and have the ability to execute task in simpler, quicker, sustainable with little or no detrimental effect to the urban environment. In this instance, the application of such intelligence in green transportation is due considered in this chapter, the role of AI in green transportation as well as challenges associated with it. While a case study of Istanbul Metropolis of Turkey was also featured.

## 11.1 Introduction

Long ago it’s through the utilization of transportation system that societies use to share their ideas, culture, and commercializing their products. Therefore, its vividly proven that, transportation is tied to our daily basic activities. Perhaps, it’s part of our culture anciently. Most of the current world developed cities rely on integrated transportation systems. While developing cities are fast growing and they cannot be free from diverse associated challenges hindering their sustainability and causing hardship to their dwellers. These challenges include air and water pollutions, soil degradation and their health and financial effects which were known to be originated form the excessive usage of fossil energy available which most conventional means of transportation is utilizing \[[1](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_1)\].

Urban environments are facing persistent problems related to pollution that expose it to different challenges. A recent study done by \[[2](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_2)\] proposes that, globally, 25% of urban ambient air pollution from Fine particulate matter (PM2.5) is being contributed to the urban environment from combustion and burning of fossil fuel by traffic and transportation activities. In this case, it should be clearly understood that road transportation contributes a significant percentage of urban environmental pollution in our cities and towns. While in most cases, air pollution causes serious problems like excessive increase of carbon monoxide (CO), global warming, ozone depletion, and likes.

As a result of the current high rise in urbanization activities, in a report by World Urbanization Prospect \[[3](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_3)\], stated that 54% of the world’s population living in an urban area and this Figure is expected to increase to 66% by 2050. The universal increase in the urbanization track is predictable that the global population will reach up to 8 billion by the year 2025 and 9 billion by 2040. Hence, this increase in universal growth is clearly considered to be occurring in urban areas whereas the total rural population is projected to remain stagnant at about 3.3 billion by 2035 and then drop slightly to 3.2 billion by 2050. All future population growth is thus urban population growth \[[4](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_4)\]. These developments are associated with the increase of vehicles ownership. Currently, world houses about 1.2 billion working vehicles (light weight vehicles, motorcycles, freight vehicles and public transport vehicles). These estimates were projected to reach 3.1 billion by the year 2050 as \[[5](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_5)\] report indicates. Clear indication has shown that a larger percentage of the above projections will be recorded from undeveloped and developing countries, because developed cities are making attempts in diversifying and encouraging green transportation missions and controlling their population.

According to World Energy Scenarios \[[6](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_6)\], the contribution that transportation made through the emission of carbon dioxide gas is up to 30% of the world total. Also, it was claimed by \[[7](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_7)\] that, transport activities are the second largest apart from energy sector in greenhouse gas (GHG) emission which contributes about 25% of the total GHG emission in the entire Europe. This amount is increasing at alarming rate which if not controlled it might cause destructive damage to the remaining ecosystem of the continent. Transportation sector is becoming larger and faster in energy consuming, despite these proven statistical data on how the transportation activities pollutes the environment through utilization of recognizable size of world’s energy in form of fossil fuel. Unfortunately, it attracts less attention of policy makers on technical means of mitigating this energy consumption. This was due to non-availability of reliable and alternative sources of non-fossil energy sources (other sources than coal, hydro, natural gas, nuclear, solar, and wind energy sources), this has become a problem as a result of over 90% of transport activities depend on fossil energy source \[[8](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_8)\].

The ideas of green transportation are encouraging to replace the current transportation vehicles on which most of them rely solely on fossil fuels as their source of energy. These fossil fuels emit considerable amount of toxic gases (carbon dioxide, CO, nitrogen oxide and other partially burnt heavy hydrocarbons (HCs)), where 23% of these toxic gases are contributed by transport sector \[[9](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_9)\]. It was discovered by Roberts & Patterson \[[10](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_10)\] that biofuel is a relatively good replacement of conventional fuels used for transport vehicles, but it has side consequences upon adoption as its major threat to food security. Therefore, these biofuels might not serve well in the nearest future.

Despite increasing proposed works on green transportation from the last two decades still no agreeable sustainable transportation systems exist between all policies makers among countries. This was resulted from the different cultural setup, environmental laws and unique nature of living standards of the societies. These hurdles have not distracted the intention of researchers aiming at developing and implementing number of marvelous green transportation policies, of course an appreciable progress was recorded. Hence, a study on green transportation, sustainable transporting or ecofriendly transportation will be just a place to start.

## 11.2 Conceptualizing Sustainable Transportation Systems

It’s a common goal for every region of the world to provide moderate energy consuming, efficient, environmental-friendly, and reliable transportation systems \[[11](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_11)\]. Developing the idea of integrating the Artificial Intelligence (AI) with generalized transportation activities being it design, analysis, construction, operation or maintenance is perhaps a key point to be needed in the current fast riding digital world. Therefore, to have consistent and continuous economic, social, and secured pollution-free environment, a deeper comprehension on how the AI could be implementable to impact into the greener transportation is prerequisite in this century. However, to have a broader understanding about the concept, there is a need to clearly have an idea about the concept of green transportation and its relationship with the sustainability and environment in general.

Sustainability is the process of developing systems that is capable of meeting the current societal demand without hindering the future basic need that will be expected from the outcome delivered by those systems \[[12](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_12)\]. The above definition is likely the most reliable and accepted definition of sustainability as it covers up visible and virtual differences such as social, economic, environmental, cultural set up, beliefs, and ethics of the societies \[[13](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_13)\]. Although this was argued that it has cover extensive broad of living which is merely complex to be adhered with. Nonetheless, at least it has captured the three triple bottom line elements of sustainable development \[[14](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_14)\]. However, considering social, economic, and environmental impact of the sustainability to the society, with this definition it can be precisely shown that sustainability cannot be complete without proper and careful consideration of transportation activities.

While Green environment as it was defined by \[[15](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_15)\] is the combination of natural and partially manmade or modified natural environment designed, control, and managed to offer uninterrupted similar services offered my naturally made ecosystem such disaster risk management, water, and air pollution mitigation, control of climate change. In addition, it includes other unique features such as terrestrial, marine areas, etc. The main aim of this combination is to provide and facilitate an enhanced quality environment to the inhabitants, serves as a barrier to biodiversity degradation and helps the ecosystem in providing social, economic, and environmental benefits. The green city or sustainable city is designed to have standard, safer, secured, and non-polluted surrounding, with additional advantage of minimal accident occurrence. Its unarguable that sustainable transport is prerequisite to a green city development as it plays a vital role to any developed city in the world. Therefore, proper integration of sustainable transport to other sector forming the green city is necessary. This synergetic approach is to be understood holistically through public-private partnership.

Additionally, green transportation may have a clear and better understanding as another term used interchangeably with sustainable transportation which refers to all transportation systems that has low negative impact of pollution emission to the environment, maintaining optimum fuel consumption, with proper considerable balance between inhabitants and environments. It also, includes a well-planned and constrained-free forecasted future of the cities based on their master plan which encompasses practices and policies, in term of economic viability, social equitability, and benefiting the environmental consistently with long term future plan without leaving any transportation gap \[[16](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_16)\]. Green/sustainable transportation system generally shows emphases on societal planning, modern technology and politics. And its main objective is to ensure an effective distribution and transit of goods, people, and the implementation of highquality transportation services in urban environments \[[17](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_17)\].

Green transportation can be viewed in two broadly perspectives either as a means of transportation, which includes walking, bicycle, regular public transport and rail transport, or from the second perspective which consider vehicles of transportation, this includes hybrid-energy vehicle, natural gas vehicle, electric vehicle, hydrogen power and solar powered vehicles \[[18](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_18)\]. The hierarchy of green transportation considering the first category was initially proposed by \[[19](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_19)\] in which they categorizes it by walking as the most effective means of attaining greener transportation, followed by bicycle, public mass transit, shared vehicles and last of this order single driving vehicles. Well integrated transportation systems tend to contribute immensely to the health, education, employments, and economic developments but it also contributes toward rapid increase of greenhouse effect due to these large fuel consumptions \[[20](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_20)\]. Its worthy to note that in this work, the sustainable transportation or green transportation as two terms were used interchangeably to relate it with sustainability ([Figure 11.1](#ch11.xhtml#fig11_1)).

![Figure 11.1 A typical prototype of green environment. Source: Adopted from \[21\].](images/fig11_1_B.jpg)

[Figure 11.1 A typical prototype of green environment.](#ch11.xhtml#R_fig11_1) *Source:* Adopted from \[[21](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_21)\].

As most of the technical systems are having their independent features and rules likewise, sustainable transportation is also related inclusively. These rules were established by \[[22](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_22)\] and they are as follows:

1.  **Reduced Travel Needs:** This has to do with establishing a relationship between information communication technology and transportation such as supporting the idea of online bus booking, online shopping, etc.
2.  **Transport Policy Shift:** This has to do with Paradigm shifts to new strategies by replacing the excessive use of cars with walking and cyclic means of movements. In this regard walking and cyclic means of transportation should be given priority especially in urban areas followed by public transport and least with conventional transport system which car usage is dominant.
3.  **Distance Reduction:** This has to do with planning new cities roadways where an accessibility and mobility will be provided to ease the difficulties for the road users in accessing the cities services (housing, commerce, recreation, and education facilities). This rule encourages the establishment of green urban island where it’s declared as no car free zone.
4.  **Integration of Artificial Intelligence (AI) to the System:** This has to do with the adopting of new technologies in vehicles manufacturing, alternatives fuels, increase in renewable energy sources, timely accessibility to major cities centers, etc. This rules also includes action toward rational drivers’ behaviors, strict traffic rules and eco-friendly vehicles provision.

## 11.3 Why Green Transportation?

Apart from the contribution offered by transportation activities for the rapid urban development recorded by most of the developed nations, these activities also contributed immensely toward other challenges associated with the urban development. These challenges include relatively high traffic growth, increased nuisance of traffic in high populated area, excessive release of converted fossil energy into poisonous gases to the environments, which in turn causes air pollution, increasing travel time of the commuters while at the same time increasing the energy usage and persistent environmental degradation. Among, these are the few challenges that sustainable transportation are targeted to solve. The solution to abovementioned challenges can be put into practice through the adaptation of proper and coherent environmental assessment, optimistic, and resilient infrastructural financing and minimizing the amount of CO[2], through encouraging the usage of other alternative means of transportation \[[23](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_23)\].

Unarguably, transportation systems are socio-technical in nature, thus dealing with such non-autonomous, complex, and diverse system is merely difficult considering the number of factors having featured variabilities it houses \[[24](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_24)\]. For this reason, some regions have already embarked on proper sensitizing local communities on the consequences of misusing these systems in accordance with their initial purposes \[[25](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_25)\]. Therefore, proper implementation of green transportation contributes not only to lower the carbon emission and provide environmental-friendly means of transportation, but also aids in promoting health through increasing the level of air quality and decreasing the amount of energy consumption.

Its worthy to note that millions of lives were claimed by road accidents annually across the world, most of were caused by cars. With this Figure in 2007, China has established and implemented prompt decision by providing transit-oriented development (TOD), a scheme which discourage the persistent use of cars as the most commonly used means of mobility in urban areas by attracting communities toward a mass transit aiming at achieving high quality life \[[26](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_26)\]. The scheme has impacted immensely toward achieving the intended goal of green transportation development. Therefore, encouraging the adaptation of sustainable transportation will fragmentally reduce the occurrence of such menace to a minimal level.

As the second largest contributor of the green gas emission, transportation contributes negatively to the environment \[[27](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_27)\]. Decoupling transportation and CO[2] is necessary through possible mitigation measures such as employing consistent sustainable measures \[[28](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_28)\]. Of course, considering other parameters of sustainable development, the rate of implementing green transportation is slower with respect to reducing the level of toxic gases emissions \[[29](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_29)\]. Regardless of all the efforts made in attaining most the sustainable goals of quality of life, basic freedom to all, accessible education by all, food security, good health care, other social capabilities, but sustainable transportation has less attention from the policies maker’s despite being one of the key drivers to the nation’s development. Therefore, special and independent care needs to be imposed on sustainable transportation. This is because human mobility and development are two non-separable phenomena, and at the same if not controlled to a certain level, over mobility will serve as destruction to the development \[[30](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_30)\].

The critical argument about the components of sustainable development by the participants of \[[31](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_31)\], it was concluded that a special attention should be drawn toward transportation and mobility as they gear up and serve as the central system of sustainable development. They also provide linkage of urban-rural areas, enhancing the level of productivity and provide economic and social improvement. Therefore, during that historic conference, it was agreed a new policymaking system should be adopted at every level of governance to adhere with the conference recommendations on sustainable transportation \[[32](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_32)\].

## 11.4 Setbacks of Conventional Transport Systems

All the tried attempts were meant to protect and improve the environment from the negative impacts of transportation. These impacts have been categorized into three by \[[33](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_33)\]:

1.  **Direct Impacts:** These are the main effects that could easily be seen and noticed, and understood such as noise sound and CO emission.
2.  **Indirect Impacts:** The consequences of these are more likely not to be understood easily, they may have direct link to respiratory problem and other cardiovascular diseases such effects are mostly caused due to incomplete gaseous combustion of internal combustion engine.
3.  **Cumulative or Tertiary Impacts:** These are having direct effects to the climate change, global warming and other natural consequences such as carbon dioxide emissions.

On boarder term, below are some of the challenges from conventional transportation systems:

1.  **Reducing the Environmental Air Quality:** Poisonous gases released by the vehicles affect the human health and natural build environment, more than a century since the inception of formal transportation systems into existence. Respiratory related diseases are escalating such as asthma, bronchitis, heart attacks, etc., were claimed to be associated with transport emitted gas which was epidemiologically reported by \[[34](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_34)\], these respiratory related diseases lead to the death of more than half of million people annually. Also report by \[[35](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_35)\] claimed that an approximate death of about 2 million is recorded across the world due to the air pollution diseases. Apart from the aforementioned effects, the excessive usage of fossil fuels as a source of energy causes depletion of natural resources, global warming, and environmental degradation.
2.  **Noise and Vibration:** Some part of the human nervous system is affected densely by excessive noise. Among problems caused by the noise and vibration from vehicles operating sound, horns, and other sounds associated with vehicles are pain and hearing fatigue, sleep disturbance, cardiovascular effects, immune system impairment, etc. It also affects underage speedy and steady leaning assimilation \[[36](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_36)\].
3.  **Accident Occurrence:** It was estimated that over 1.2 million lives are usually accumulated by accident annually across the world, with more than 50 million injuries. These estimates are mostly accounted for in undeveloped and developing countries \[[37](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_37)\]. It was projected the road accident will be the fifth leading cause of death by the year 2030 \[[38](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_38)\].
4.  **Global Climate Change:** As it was mentioned before, more than a quarter of the fossil-based carbon dioxide released to the atmosphere was sourced from vehicles. In 2009, transport was responsible for 23% of global carbon dioxide emissions from fossil-fuel-based sources, while the energy sector accounted for 41% of these emissions \[[39](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_39)\]. However, by 2035, transport is expected to become the single largest GHG emitter, accounting for 46% of global GHG emissions \[[40](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_40)\].
5.  **Disruption of Naturally Build Environment:** Road and other related transport systems construction is associated with the depletion naturally build environment.
6.  **Traffic Congestion:** It is inevitable to the delay of the general activities of the road users. It was estimated by \[[41](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_41)\] that as for 2010, United Stated of America losses about $101 billion as result of traffic congestion majorly Resulted from the fuel wasted during the delay and time spend by passengers. Also, Thailand’s GDP was shrinkage by about 6% as a result of congestion experienced on the Bangkok city’s major roadways \[[42](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_42)\]. According to the research conducted by \[[43](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_43)\], the lost cause by congestion in China cities constitute about 2% of its GDP.
7.  **Fossil Energy Security:** Continuous usage of petrol-based fuels by a particular country may lead to the perishing of its natural resource which in the long run might expose the nation to the energy-insecurity stage. While to the countries relying on importing fuel related product may be threatened with price hike and invariably impacts and experienced shortfalls on their national treasury.

## 11.5 Possible Solutions

To curb these aforementioned challenges, many developed cities have introduced technological leapfrogging, i.e., the idea of sustainable transportation through series programs encouraging the usage of alternative means of transportation which include electric cars, improving the usage of commercial buses as replacement of commonly private cars which intended to have high fuel consumption cost maintenance cost as well as contributes in polluting the environment. Also \[[44](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_44)\] claims that for a city to implement the idea of green transportation it has to follow the series of activities without skip these are: basic access stage – (provision of local/access road) paratransit dominant stage (availability of public transportation), early motorization stage (provision of means of transportation means), full motorization stage (availability of public and private transportation means), quality of life stage (integrated and effective public transport systems). However, \[[45](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_45)\] stated that since the 1992 Earth Summit in Rio, when Agenda 21 which sustainable transportation is inclusive and many cities such as Amsterdam (Netherlands), Bogota (Colombia), Brisbane (Australia), Copenhagen (Denmark), Curitiba (Brazil), Guangzhou (People’s Republic of China (PRC), Portland (United States), and Seoul (Republic of Korea), have embraced implementing the idea.

### 11.5.1 Electric Vehicles (EVS)

The most challenging and accepted fact in the nearest future is that the world oil reservation will keep declining, while the rate of its consumption will comparatively rise as a result of factors earlier mentioned. Therefore, to avoid necessary disruption of transportation sector which heavily rely on the oil production, its requisite to implement other innovative technological advancements to replace the over dependence on oil as the source of its energy. This can be achieved through the introduction and adaptation of proposed green transportation schemes such as electric and hybrid vehicles as it reported by \[[46](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_46)\]. These vehicles (electric vehicles, hybrid electric vehicles, plug-in hybrid electric vehicles) are capable of minimizing the negative effects caused by the conventional vehicles \[[21](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_21)\]. Although, the idea of EVs has faced many challenges from its inception to date as many developed and developing nations in collaboration with well-developed manufacturing companies such as spaceX (TESLA) in USA, Nissan (LEAF) in Hong Kong Tata in India, etc., are still struggling to be accepted by the societies.

In order to minimize these challenges faced by EVs, the Hong Kong government has installed about 300 charging points across 18 districts. While China along as the leader of the electric vehicle production in the world are having more than half of million EVs across the country. The Nissan leaf product as the first type of electric vehicle can travel at upper range speed of 145 kM/hr. and the charging can last for 160 KM. It is not astonishing that the production of EVs was dated back to 1838 before the inception of combustion engine \[[47](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_47)\]. The comfortability and massive commercialization of these combustion engine in later years brought about the drop of the EVs. Afterward, several attempts have been made to reintroduce the EVs which most of these attempts failed due to many factors. But based on the recent progress and support could made more than 50% of the total sales by the year 2050. Making up to 25% of the world vehicles sales ([Figure 11.2](#ch11.xhtml#fig11_2)).

![Figure 11.2 Typical charging process of electric car. Source: Adopted from \[48\].](images/fig11_2_B.jpg)

[Figure 11.2 Typical charging process of electric car.](#ch11.xhtml#R_fig11_2) *Source:* Adopted from \[[48](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_48)\].

Another contribution made by The International Renewable Energy Agency (IRENA) \[[49](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_49)\] as an advancement to the green transportation are the recommendations of implementing EVs and adoption of biofuels. This campaigned recommendation has encouraged the usage of EVs at the end of 2016 to more than 2 million across the world. Again, much is also needed to speed up the process. With regard to biofuels, several research have shown that they will serve the same purpose with the conventional energy sources (gasoline, diesel, and kerosene) available to internal combustion engines. The International Renewable Energy Agency \[[49](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_49)\] suggested that upon adopted at least 10% of the energy exhausted by transportation sector will be saved by the year 2030. However, it has negative consequences as it was mentioned in the beginning of this work.

### 11.5.2 Car Free Zone

The idea of care free-zone has been adopted and been in practice in some developing cities such as Buenos Aires (Argentina), Cartagena (Colombia), Curitiba (Brazil), Old Delhi (India), Quito (Ecuador), Sao Paulo (Brazil), and Shanghai (PRC) have permanent car-free areas, as another stage to improve and support the idea of green transportation.

### 11.5.3 Greenways

A recent development aiming at replacing the conventional, dominated, and motorized landscape with green plantation in most pedestrianized runways was greenways, apart from its initial purpose of improving the green transportation system, they also serve as recreational zones in cities.

### 11.5.4 Improving Public Transport

It’s difficult to reduce the usage level of conventional means of transportation through alternative means such as massive deployment of public transportation, bicycling, walking. Despite all the challenges associated with these means it impacts less on the environment based on pollution reduction, fuel costs, economic growth and building strong social networking. By the way considering the active implementation of overall green transport integration in different forms to transport services, it will improve the attractiveness and convenience of public transport. Having implemented the idea of green transportation, it is also necessary to consider remodeling the current system of public transport in order to attract public attention to it and bridging the competition of the private car owner ship among the commuters.

Urban road-based strategies can be implemented as proposed strategies: they include bus rapid transit (BRT) paratransit upgrades, urban railbased, these includes streets trams, at grade light rail transit (LRT) elevated light rail transit (ELRT), metro, proper consideration of fare system which can be done by online booking, as well as keening to promote the use of smart ticketing which allows passengers to move seamlessly between different modes \[[50](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_50)\]. Another means of attracting public attention to the less energy-consumption means of transportation is that governments must be ready to modify public transport systems by increasing the level of service, proper parking facilities provision, an affordable fare to the commuters. Provision of integrated routes network purposely for public transportation, restructuring the stations in term of comfortability, security, conveniences, optimizing the taxation system, and provision of terminals along major crowded places such as market for simple loading and unloading goods and services, as well as integration of these routes and terminals with freight vehicles routes, feeder service and other related mass transit systems such linkage to airports, seaports, and rail terminals needed for urban development \[[51](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_51)\].

### 11.5.5 Smart Cities Establishments

Although the entire sustainable transportation system is part of the smart cities agenda. But nonetheless rapid evolvement of the smart cities will jointly provide assured means of proper green transport systems.

### 11.5.6 Eco-Friendly Urban Schemes

Most of the developed cities in the world are threatened with the large volume of toxic gaseous emission from both mobile and static sources. Therefore, an advanced means of reducing such menace is by encouraging eco-friendly innovation. These includes provision of cyclic tracks, BRT system (BRT) intelligent transport system, urban freight management, hybridization of fossil fuel and non-fossils fuel vehicles.

### 11.5.7 Green Traveling Awareness and Advocates

The already implemented active green transportation in use by most of the cities in the world lacks public participation despite the support offered by the government. Therefore, active promotion of green traveling through adopting functional sensitization programs which can be implemented by the support of schools, local communities, advocates groups, environmental protection organizations, transportation actors, and voluntary organizations solely for green transportation. These programs should obtain full support from the government through adopting flexible administrative, economic, and technological approaches and further integration of the system with guided and updated scientific research. This advocate can be embedded with prompt initiatives from NGOs and collaboration with all the relevant bodies \[[52](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_52)\].

### 11.5.8 Provision of Long-Term Resilience System

The problems associated with the most models developed is nonresiliencies after a short period of initial implementation. Proper design of long-term sustained mechanisms will help green transportation to be part of the public culture. This can be achieved by adopting intelligent transport system, integrated road networks, accessible mobility, policies on energy conservations and environmental protection \[[18](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_18)\]. In addition, a well-planned and oriented-transit and zoning, developing reliable policies that favor the land use, development of land belt and urban boundaries will assist in longing the scheme.

### 11.5.9 Tele-Mobility

Avoidance or reducing of unnecessary trips using new paradigms of implementing green transportation is another way in which the use of information technology as a replacement of some manageable activities through the adoption of TOD. This focused on adopting of these sophisticated components for tele-mobility which include telecommuting, telework, teleconferencing, teleshopping, and telemedicine. In broader term the tele-mobility is component of AI which discourage excessive travel demand \[[53](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_53)\].

### 11.5.10 Restoration of Climate

The sustainable transportation system contributes immensely toward improving the cleaner environment. Much attention has been given to this sector recently by most of the countries through public-private partnership, which has now become inevitable to the economic and social development. Transportation sector can be supported by the implementation of policies, appraisal, and managerial commitment by the parties responsible.

The global warming is far beyond the individual or community problem but rather world ones. Although it can be sub divided into different levels depending on the laws and regional cultural setup. Among these initiatives were global fuel economy initiatives (GFEI) \[[54](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_54)\]. This was initiated to control and improve the fuel consumption management, one of their recent reports claims that carbon dioxide will drastically reduce to half in the next three decades, while more than 5 million barrels of oil could be saved annually. The (GFEI) was formed by collaboration between united nation environment program \[[55](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_55)\] and international transport forum and federation international automobile foundation.

1.  **The Mobility Model Partnership:** Popularly known as MoMo, this model was developed to have a regional-based database of world transport, the model contains mode of transportation, fuel consumption, regions, previous record of transportation of the region as input variables and provides the next decade’s prediction of energy consumed by transportation activities based on quantitative analysis using these parameters. The successful implementation of this model has brought about future forecast of energy use, vehicle, and fuel cost, as well as GHG emission.
2.  **The Electric Vehicles (EVs) Initiative:** Although it was initiated long before by different countries through the public-private partnership, it has experienced set back from some countries due to the lack of enabling environment.
3.  **Sustainable and Low Carbon Transport Partnership:** The drawback of implementing the most sustainable transportation policies is recorded and experienced by developing countries. Due to this problem, this partnership was established to serve as sensitization and knowledge sharing medium to these countries affected. This partnership includes more than 50 organizations amongst are United nation, NGOs, research institutions, development banks and private agencies.
4.  **Urban Sustainability Framework:** This was initially developed by \[[56](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_56)\] as the strategized transportation planning measures focused on evaluating the green transport performance based on the triple bottom line of sustainability (economy viability, social equitability and environmental maintainability) to monitor the green transportation track using household, market access point, land use and local road are parametric input measures.

## 11.6 The Impact of Implementing Green Transportation to Socio-Economic Development

In consideration of economic benefits, green transportation hugely permits savings and reduce the construction and maintenance cost due to its borrowed natural nature, for instance, storm run-off that vacates from road surface usually pollutes water bodies, causing health and financial distress to inhabitants. Direct replacement of these conventional roadways and another structural component of highways with green transportation systems assists in significant reduction of this cost. In addition, porous pavement was found to reduce almost 25% of the cost, if it is replaced with the conventional pavement as \[[57](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_57)\] claims. Although the live span of standard pervious pavement might last up to 25 working years if proper maintenance was adopted.

Another important point which needs to be considered offered by green transportation is the reviving socio-economic sector through creation of tourism attraction sites. Relevant design and construction methods such as frequent usage of permeable pavements which in turns reduces management cost and environmental pollution and maintaining the ecological buildup within the landscape and massively reduces the traffic congestion. Again, the entire transport systems and other infrastructures are prone to destruction by extreme weather conditions as well as unexpected natural disasters such as floods, landslides which normally cost lives and millions of dollars annually. One of the proposed solutions to reduce such destructive phenomenon was the provision of green bridges and eco tunnels \[[58](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_58)\]. Green embankments integrated along roads also serves as efficient noise barrier, help in pollution reduction through filtering the storm-water flows.

Furthermore, green construction is adopted with the integration of vegetated drainage, porous materials, permeable pavers, into streets or parking lots \[[59](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_59)\]. Therefore, integrating green transportation into sustainable environment will help in reducing carbon footprint, neutralize the effects caused by it on land, water, and other features within the environment. The provision of green transportation will also help in increasing its residences to the disaster ([Figures 11.3](#ch11.xhtml#fig11_3) and [11.4](#ch11.xhtml#fig11_4)).

![Figure 11.3 Permeable pavement. Source: Adopted from \[60\].](images/fig11_3_B.jpg)

![Figure 11.4 Green bridges and eco-tunnels. Source: Adopted from \[60\].](images/fig11_4_B.jpg)

## 11.7 Introducing and Implementing the Concept of Greener Logistics

Ahead of summing up the solution to the challenges of conventional transportation, it’s vital to include the concept of green logistics, due to its functional impact to the economy and aiding in friendly environment development. Freight transportation which most of the logistics activities rely upon, contribute immensely to polluting the environment, causing harm to the reserved natural resources through depleting the fossil fuels sources \[[61](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_61)\]. Green logistics encompasses the distribution, energy consumption, manufacturing, production, and raw material mining \[[62](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_62)\]. Therefore, overlapping the Intelligent transport system to it will improve the entire activities of the logistics processes through provision of safe, less-energy consumption, and affordable systems.

## 11.8 The Role of Artificial Intelligence (Ai) in Green/Sustainable Transportation

Inevitably, there is need of overlapping green transportation with AI, because the rapid growth recoded by the AI in many fields within the first two decades of 21^(st) century reaffirms it. The sole aim of AI in sustainable transportation is to avoid the manual or human maintenances to transportation demand while at the same time improve the energy-usage efficiency through the introduction of new technology. Therefore, deployment of AI in green transportation will lead to have another aspect which termed as smart greener transportation. Many countries have embarked on developing smart cities and smart mobility, some of these technologies includes:

1.  **Geographical Positioning System (GPS) Application:** Inclusion of GPS to monitor the activities of transportation within the city via the usage of sophisticated and smart devices, controlling, and management of the drivers, passengers, other road user behavior’s will have enhanced the security and reduce the traffic and risk of accident on the road. While, vehicles routes, fare collection, smart taxing, can also be attained with little or no hindrance if the system is properly implemented and managed.
2.  **Smart Traffic System:** The application of smart traffic system can be used to link vehicles within specific designed area for detecting and mitigating the sensed traffic congestion and averted it through alternative routes. This can be used as they can automatically present the available routes for the drivers. The idea will help in saving energy, time, and reduce the travel time, while adding efficiency to the systems by reducing the toxic gaseous emission to the environment. They will also alert drivers on the potentialities of exceeding speed limits within urban areas \[[63](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_63)\].
3.  **Smart Streetlight:** These are sophisticated forms of streetlights that have a ranged regulatory that normally detect the presence of vehicles or other road users using the mounted sensors. They are usually adopted in local streets with less congestion to save energy \[[64](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_64)\].
4.  **Operational Management Systems:** Another paradigm for implementation of the AI in the sustainable transportation is by adopting operational management systems to support the decision support system of transportation, these support systems can be used to smoothly analyze and provide relevant information of the systems needed such information includes traffic flow data, road accidents data, etc. Based on the data collected a proper planning can be laid. For these operational management different AI approaches such as data mining, genetic algorithm (GA), artificial neural network, evolutionary computation, situational analysis, simulation, and advanced modeling, reasoning based on precedents, retrieval knowledge from the database were employed as \[[65](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_65)\] explicitly explained. Although these operational management activities need very experienced personnel for the collection, analysis, presentation, and retrieval of data. While the adequacy and quality of the data can help immensely toward the simulation process.

## 11.9 Challenges Associated with Green Transportation

For a reliable and working systems to exist it must be associated with different challenge, likewise this idea of green transportation is of no exception in this regard. Below are some challenges of sustainable transportation:

1.  **Public Sensitization, Awareness, and Participation:** Hindrances and setback factors to most government policies are lacking awareness from the receiving end, therefore different awareness program should be set by government at different levels to encourage and promote public acceptance and participation in green transportation adaptations. Although, it may be difficult to some region due their lower literacy level. The level of awareness lacking between the stakeholders is another factor hindering the implementing the core aim of green transport. Therefore, knowledge sharing, and idea transmission will help in bridging the gap.
2.  **Non-Availability of Smart Changing Stations:** Green vehicles and charging stations are inseparable, the abundance of charging stations and rate of charging should be considered while designing the system. In most cases the electric and hybrid vehicles will be affected much by this. Installations of such charging stations should be based on user preference for easy attraction of public into the system.
3.  **Power Storage:** The power source of both electric and hybrid vehicles is battery, and these batteries are associated with some setback attributes. These are heavy and expensive power storage, longer charging period, toxicity in nature as it contains heavy element which will pollute environment if not properly disposed. Lack of detective control system for overcharging and undercharging the battery is also part of the challenges.
4.  **Non-Secured Walkways Facilities:** Despite its advantage to low income earners, non-motorized transport (NMT) (walking and bicycling) transport is less safe and conveniently non-attractive. Because of less barrier to extreme high levels of noise and air pollution from the nearby motorized roads, exposed to hazardous level of high-speed traffic, lack of properly and well-designed crossing facilities, insecurity at some certain sections which leads to criminal acts such as robbery.
5.  **Funding and Other Political Supports:** Lack of funding is another challenge faced by the processes of green transportation. Therefore, proper provision of necessarily needed funding which was proven to be less when compared to the conventional methods employed, and practical implementation will be among the priority concerns to all decision makers responsible for green transportation adaption.

## 11.10 Ai-Based Sustainable Transportation (Case Study of Istanbul Metropolis Turkey)

### 11.10.1 The Framework of the Case Study

In trying to achieve the goal of this research, a questionnaire samples were established with 307 responses which were used to evaluate and measure citizen’s participation in using green transportation systems with the values they seem to attach in making the environment a safer one. This study chose the city of Istanbul, Turkey as the major case study to obtain the related answers and information needed for the research. The response and results were analyzed and presented in the form of Figures representing the respondent’s opinions in different sections.

The questionnaire contains two major sections – in the first section, the demographic, social, and economic characteristics of the respondents were surveyed. This was done to assess the level of the social and demographic values of the respondents as a basic indicator to evaluate their need for a sustainable transportation system. While the second section, the respondents’ understanding of the term green transportation, respondent quality of life, the exposure of green transportation facilities to the respondents were asked and reviewed as the indicators that can give the information of the significance of using the green form of the transportation system.

### 11.10.3 Second Section: Suitability of Adapting Artificial Intelligence (Ai)-Based Green Transportation

From the data recorded and analysis conducted, it can be concluded that most of the existing transportation modes are being operated with the use of fossil fuels such as gas, oil, coal, etc., to power the engine for operation. But when considering AI -based green transportation systems, it is among the key factor for having a sustainable green environment with minimum damages from human daily activities. This smart transportation system (STS) sensitizes the environment from any future detriment that might likely arise due to human activities and makes it friendly with nature. A mere example of the significance of using the green transportation system can be seen from the respondent’s opinions in using these green forms of transportation. For example, using an electric car that requires no burning of fuel for its operation will save the environment from the excess emission of the car exhaust to the atmosphere which will reduce the level of pollution of our environment ([Figure 11.11](#ch11.xhtml#fig11_11), [Figure 11.12](#ch11.xhtml#fig11_12), [Figure 11.13](#ch11.xhtml#fig11_13), [Figure 11.14](#ch11.xhtml#fig11_14), [Figure 11.15](#ch11.xhtml#fig11_15), [Figure 11.16](#ch11.xhtml#fig11_16), [Figure 11.17](#ch11.xhtml#fig11_17), [Figure 11.18](#ch11.xhtml#fig11_18), [Figure 11.19](#ch11.xhtml#fig11_19) and [Figure 11.20](#ch11.xhtml#fig11_20)).

The use of bicycles by people in the urban areas instead of driving personal cars to work, school, or marketplaces will both increase the health condition of the people as well as the environment. Using green trains in place of coal trains, walk in a walkable distance to school, grocery stores or markets will also improve the health of people and their environment. In general terms, the use of these green transportation systems has a great impact on the sustainable environment by making it a pollution-free urban environment, improve the health condition of people, boost economic and individual resources by using the less money defendant green facilities among others.

## 11.11 Conclusion

People living in the urban areas shall aim at developing sustainable transport systems. This will enable them to achieve the appropriate level of growth both in economic, social, and environmental spheres. Because, green transportation advocates the decreased use of private motor cars, the increased use of walking, bicycle, and public transport, and the use of clean energies and vehicles. It is also a low-cost, pollution-free, land resource and space saving transportation system suitable for all kinds of travelers.

Lack of proper implementation is the most challenging task toward achieving the goals of sustainable transportation, these challenges were hindered by mere regional, cultural, and in most cases by political reasons. Therefore, better understanding of these factors causing the setback is much important, these can be achieved through public consultation of the concerned communities, regulating the current traffic policies, pricings, and managements to suite the targeted sustainability requirements as well as the consideration of cultural setup of the concerned communities. These proposed measures toward implementation will probably take time and need resources, which in turn will avoid unreasonable delay leading to the collapse of the system in general. Political support perhaps is crucial toward the implementation of sustainable transportation ideas to the optimal required level, while effective and consistent enforcement is an essential part of implementation. Although, sometimes enforcement found to be problematic, where enforcement proves problematic, modification of the regulation, charge, etc., can be used to render enforcement \[[66](#ch11.xhtml#ref11_66)\].
